<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
Let $p\in [1,\infty)$ and $\gamma\in \mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}$.
For any $u\in L^p(Q_0)$, define
\begin{align*}
m_{p,\gamma}(u):=\inf\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(u_j;Q_0),
\end{align*}
where the infimum is taken over all sequences
$\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ and
$\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(Q_0)$ satisfying
$u_j\to u$ in $L^p(Q_0)$, with
$\lambda_j\to\infty$ if $\gamma\in(0,\infty)$ and $\lambda_j\to0$ if $\gamma\in(-\infty,0)$.
Moreover, let
\begin{align}\label{baochang}
C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}:=m_{p,\gamma}(\ell)
\end{align}
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm-negdom}
Let $N\ge1$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1]\setminus\{-p\}$, and
$\Omega\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded open interval when $N=1$ or a
bounded Lipschitz domain when $N\geq2$.
Then the family $F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\cdot;\Omega)$ converges, in the sense
of $\Gamma$-convergence in $L^p(\Omega)$, to
$\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\Omega}$ as $\lambda\to0^+$.
More precisely, let $\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$
satisfy $\lambda_j\to0$. Then the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[{\rm(i)}]
\item The $\Gamma$-liminf inequality: for any $u\in L^p(\Omega)$ and any
sequence $\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(\Omega)$ satisfying
$u_j\to u$ in $L^p(\Omega)$,
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(u_j;\Omega)
\geq\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\Omega}(u).
\end{align*}
\item The $\Gamma$-limsup inequality: for any $u\in L^p(\Omega)$, there
exists a sequence $\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(\Omega)$ satisfying
$u_j\to u$ in $L^p(\Omega)$ and
\begin{align*}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(u_j;\Omega)
\leq\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\Omega}(u).
\end{align*}
Such a sequence is called a \emph{recovery sequence} for $u$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm-domfull}
Let  $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\gamma\in\mathbb R\setminus\{0\}$, and
$\Omega\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded open interval when $N=1$ or a
bounded Lipschitz domain when $N\geq2$.
Then the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[{\rm(i)}]
\item If $\gamma\in(0,\infty)$, then the family
$F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\cdot;\Omega)$ converges, in the sense of
$\Gamma$-convergence in $L^p(\Omega)$, to $\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\Omega}$ as
$\lambda\to\infty$.
\item If $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1]$, then the family
$F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\cdot;\Omega)$ converges, in the sense of
$\Gamma$-convergence in $L^p(\Omega)$, to $\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\Omega}$ as
$\lambda\to0^+$.
\item If $\gamma\in(-1,0)$, then then the family
$F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\cdot;\Omega)$ converges, in the sense of
$\Gamma$-convergence in $L^p(\Omega)$, to the zero functional as
$\lambda\to0^+$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm-whlfull}
Let  $p\in[1,\infty)$ and $\gamma\in\mathbb R\setminus\{0\}$.
Define the functional
$\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}:L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)\to[0,\infty]$ by
setting, for any $u\in L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$,
\begin{align*}
\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}(u)
:=
\begin{cases}
C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb R^N}|\nabla u|^p\,dx,
&p\in(1,\infty)\ \hbox{and}\ u\in\dot W^{1,p}(\mathbb R^N),\\[2mm]
C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\rm cell}|Du|(\mathbb R^N),
&p=1,\ (N,\gamma)\neq(1,-1),\ \hbox{and}\ u\in\dot{BV}(\mathbb R^N),\\[2mm]
C_{1,1,-1}^{\rm cell}\left(|Du|(\mathbb R)+|u_+-u_-|\right),
&(N,p,\gamma)=(1,1,-1)\ \hbox{and}\ u\in\dot{BV}(\mathbb R),\\[1mm]
\infty,&\hbox{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\end{align*}
Then the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[{\rm(i)}]
\item If $\gamma\in(0,\infty)$, then the family
$F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\cdot;\mathbb R^N)$ converges, in the sense of
$\Gamma$-convergence in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$, to
$\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}$ as $\lambda\to\infty$.
\item If $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1]$, then the family
$F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\cdot;\mathbb R^N)$ converges, in the sense of
$\Gamma$-convergence in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$, to
$\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}$ as $\lambda\to0^+$.
\item If $\gamma\in(-1,0)$, then the family
$F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\cdot;\mathbb R^N)$ converges, in the sense of
$\Gamma$-convergence in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$, to
the zero functional as $\lambda\to0^+$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm-pliminf}
Let $p\in(1,\infty)$,
$\gamma\in(-\infty,-1]\setminus\{-p\}$, and
$\Omega\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded open interval when $N=1$ or a
bounded Lipschitz domain when $N\geq2$.
Let
$\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy
$\lambda_j\to0$, and let $\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(\Omega)$
satisfy $u_j\to u$ in $L^p(\Omega)$ for some $u\in L^p(\Omega)$. Then
\begin{align}\label{xiaji}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)
\geq C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}\int_\Omega|\nabla u|^p\,dx,
\end{align}
where the right-hand side is understood to be $\infty$ if
$u\notin W^{1,p}(\Omega)$.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm-onelinf}
Let $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1)$ and
$\Omega\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded open interval when $N=1$ or a
bounded open set when $N\geq2$. Let
$\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy
$\lambda_j\to0$, and let $\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^1(\Omega)$
satisfy $u_j\to u$ in $L^1(\Omega)$ for some $u\in L^1(\Omega)$. Then
\begin{align}\label{duanxia}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)
\geq C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\rm cell}|Du|(\Omega),
\end{align}
where the right-hand side is understood to be $\infty$ when $u\notin BV(\Omega)$.
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop-coerc}
Let  $p\in[1,\infty)$ and
$\gamma\in(-\infty,-1]\setminus\{-p\}$. Let
$\Omega\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded open interval when $N=1$ or a
bounded open set when $N\geq2$. Let
$\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy
$\lambda_j\to0$, and let $\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(\Omega)$
satisfy $u_j\to u$ in $L^p(\Omega)$ for some $u\in L^p(\Omega)$. Then the
following statements hold.
\begin{itemize}
\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] If $p\in(1,\infty)$, then
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)
\geq c_{N,p,\gamma}\int_\Omega|\nabla u|^p\,dx,
\end{align*}
where the right-hand side is understood to be $\infty$ if
$u\notin W^{1,p}(\Omega)$ and $c_{N,p,\gamma}$ is a positive constant depending only on $N,p,$ and $\gamma.$
\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] If $p=1$, then
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)
\geq c_{N,\gamma}|Du|(\Omega),
\end{align*}
where the right-hand side is understood to be $\infty$ if
$u\notin BV(\Omega)$ and $c_{N,\gamma}$ is a positive constant depending only on $N$ and $\gamma.$
\end{itemize}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
Let $p\in [1,\infty)$, $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1]\setminus\{-p\}$,
$\Omega:=Q_0$ and $u:=\ell$ in Proposition \ref{prop-coerc}. By the definition of $C^{\rm cell}_{N,p,\gamma}$ and Proposition \ref{prop-coerc}, we  find that
\begin{align*}
C^{\rm cell}_{N,p,\gamma}
=m_{p,\gamma}(\ell)
\geq
c_{N,p,\gamma}>0.
\end{align*}
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem-critosc}
Let $I\subset\mathbb R$ be a bounded open interval. Let
$\{\delta_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy
$\delta_j\to0$, and let $\{f_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^1(I)$
satisfy $f_j\to g$ in $L^1(I)$
for some $f\in L^1(I).$
Then there exists a positive
constant $c$ such that
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\delta_j,1,-1}\left(f_j;I\right)
\geq c\left(\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_I f-
\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_I f\right),
\end{align*}
where the right-hand side is understood to be $\infty$ when the essential
oscillation is infinite.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem-seqosc}
Let $p\in(1,\infty)$, $\gamma\in(-p,-1]$, and
$I\subset\mathbb R$ be a bounded  open interval. Let
$\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy
$\lambda_j\to0$, and let $\{f_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^1(I)$
satisfy $f_j\to f$ in $L^1(I)$ for some $f\in L^1(I)$.
Then there exists a positive constant $c_p$, depending only on $p$, such that
\begin{align}\label{xuzhen}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(f_j;I\right)
\geq\frac{c_p}{|I|^{p-1}}
\left(\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_If-
\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_If\right)^p,
\end{align}
where the right-hand side is understood to be $\infty$ when the essential
oscillation is infinite.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
Fix  $j\in\mathbb N$ and  let $r_j\in (0,\infty)$ be chosen later.
Since $1+\gamma/p>0$,
it follows that,
for any $x,y\in I$ with  $0<|x-y|\leq r_j$,
\begin{align*}
\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac\gamma p}
\leq\lambda_jr_j^{1+\frac\gamma p}.
\end{align*}
Consequently,
\begin{align*}
&\left\{(x,y)\in I\times I:
0<|x-y|\leq r_j,|f_j(x)-f_j(y)|>
\lambda_jr_j^{1+\frac\gamma p}\right\}\\
&\qquad\subset
\left\{(x,y)\in I\times I: x\not=y,|f_j(x)-f_j(y)|>
\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac\gamma p}\right\}.
\end{align*}
Moreover, from $\gamma+1\leq0$, we deduce that,
for any $x,y\in I$ with  $0<|x-y|\leq r_j$,
\begin{align*}
|x-y|^{\gamma-1}
=|x-y|^{\gamma+1}|x-y|^{-2}
\geq r_j^{\gamma+1}|x-y|^{-2}.
\end{align*}
Using these comparisons and the identity
\begin{align*}
\lambda_j^pr_j^{\gamma+1}
=\left(\lambda_jr_j^{\frac\gamma p}\right)^{p-1}
\lambda_jr_j^{1+\frac\gamma p},
\end{align*}
we obtain
\begin{align}\label{oscnear}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(f_j;I\right)
&=\lambda_j^p\underset{|f_j(x)-f_j(y)|>\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\gamma/p}
}{\int_I\int_I}|x-y|^{\gamma-1}\,dy\,dx\notag\\
&\geq\lambda_j^pr_j^{\gamma+1}
\underset{\substack{0<|x-y|\leq r_j\\
|f_j(x)-f_j(y)|>\lambda_jr_j^{1+\gamma/p}}}{\int_I\int_I}
\frac{dy\,dx}{|x-y|^2}.
\end{align}
Let $\delta_j:=\lambda_jr_j^{1+\gamma/p}.$
Then
\begin{align}\label{huiwu}
F_{\delta_j,1,-1}\left(f_j;I\right)=\delta_j
\underset{\substack{0<|x-y|\leq r_j\\
|f_j(x)-f_j(y)|>\delta_j}}{\int_I\int_I}
\frac{dy\,dx}{|x-y|^2}+\delta_j
\underset{\substack{|x-y|>r_j\\
|f_j(x)-f_j(y)|>\delta_j}}{\int_I\int_I}
\frac{dy\,dx}{|x-y|^2}
=:E_j+F_j.
\end{align}
Note that
\begin{align*}
F_j&\leq\delta_j\int_I\int_{\left\{y\in\mathbb R:|x-y|>r_j\right\}}
\frac{dy\,dx}{|x-y|^2}=2|I|\lambda_jr_j^{\frac \gamma p}.
\end{align*}
By this, \eqref{huiwu}, and  \eqref{oscnear}, we conclude that
\begin{align}\label{osccomp}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(f_j;I\right)\geq
\lambda_j^{p-1}r_{j}^{\gamma-\frac\gamma p}E_j\geq\lambda_j^{p-1}r_{j}^{\gamma-\frac \gamma p}
\left[F_{\delta_j,1,-1}\left(f_j;I\right)-2|I|\lambda_jr_j^{\frac\gamma p}\right].
\end{align}
If $\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_If-\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_If=0$,
then \eqref{xuzhen} holds automatically. Let
\begin{align*}
0<A<\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_If-
\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_If\leq\infty.
\end{align*}
Now, we choose $r_j$ such that $2|I|\lambda_jr_j^{\gamma/p}=\frac{cA}{2}.$ Then
\begin{align*}
r_j=\left(\frac{4|I|\lambda_j}{cA}\right)^{-\frac p \gamma}\to 0
\ \ \text{and}\ \ \delta_j= \lambda_jr_j^{1+\frac\gamma p}
=\frac{cA}{4|I|}r_j\to0
\end{align*}
as $j\to\infty$. From this and  \eqref{osccomp}, we infer that
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(f_j;I\right)\geq\left(\frac{cA}{4|I|}\right)^{p-1}
\left(F_{\delta_j,1,-1}(f_j;I)-\frac{cA}{2}\right).
\end{align*}
Letting $j\to\infty$ in the above inequality and using  Lemma \ref{lem-critosc},  we find that
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(f_j;I\right)
\geq \left(\frac{cA}{4|I|}\right)^{p-1}
\left(cA-\frac{cA}{2}\right)=\frac{c^p}{2\cdot4^{p-1}}\frac{A^p}{|I|^{p-1}}.
\end{align*}
Letting $A\to \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_If-
\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_If$ in the estimate as above, we then conclude that \eqref{xuzhen} holds. This finishes
the proof of Lemma \ref{lem-seqosc}.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem-diffbd}
Let $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\gamma\in(-\infty,-p)$, and $I:=(a,b)$
be a bounded  open interval. Then, for any $g\in L^\infty(I)$,
 $\lambda\in(0,\infty)$, and  $r\in(0,\frac{|I|}{2})$,
\begin{align}\label{chashang}
&\int_r^{2r}\int_a^{b-h}
\left|\frac{g(x+h)-g(x)}h\right|^p\,dx\,\frac{dh}h
\leq\frac{2^{-\gamma}-1}{2}F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(g;I)
+\frac{2^\gamma-1}{\gamma}\lambda^p|I|r^\gamma.
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Let $g\in L^\infty(I)$, $\lambda\in(0,\infty)$, and  $r\in(0,\frac{|I|}{2})$. Then
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(g;I)
=2\lambda^p\int_0^{|I|}h^{\gamma-1}\int_a^{b-h}
\mathbf1_{\{|g(x+h)-g(x)|>\lambda h^{1+\gamma/p}\}}\,dx\,dh.
\end{align*}
Let
$s_+:=\max\{s,0\}$ for any $s\in\mathbb R$.
Then, for any $t\in\mathbb{R}$ and $h\in (0,\infty)$,
\begin{align*}
|t|^p
\leq\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma}
+\left(|t|^p-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma}\right)_+.
\end{align*}
Consequently,
\begin{align*}
&\int_r^{2r}\int_a^{b-h}
\left|\frac{g(x+h)-g(x)}h\right|^p\,dx\,\frac{dh}h\\
&\quad\leq\int_r^{2r}\int_a^{b-h}
\frac{(|g(x+h)-g(x)|^p-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma})_+}{h^{p+1}}
\,dx\,dh
+\frac{2^\gamma-1}{\gamma}\lambda^p|I|r^\gamma.
\end{align*}
Thus, to prove \eqref{chashang}, it suffices  to show that
\begin{align}\label{wangji}
\int_r^{2r}\int_a^{b-h}
\frac{(|g(x+h)-g(x)|^p-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma})_+}{h^{p+1}}
\,dx\,dh
\leq\frac{2^{-\gamma}-1}{2}F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(g;I).
\end{align}
Observe that, for any $A,B\in\mathbb{R}$
with $|A+B|^p>\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma}$
and $h\in (0,\infty)$,
\begin{align*}
 &|A+B|^p-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma}\\
&\quad\leq2^{p-1}(|A|^p+|B|^p)-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma}\\
&\quad=
2^{p-1}(|A|^p-\lambda^p(h/2)^{p+\gamma})
+2^{p-1}(|B|^p-\lambda^p(h/2)^{p+\gamma})+(2^{-\gamma}-1)\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma}.
\end{align*}
Thus,
for any $A,B\in\mathbb R$ and
$h\in(0,\infty)$,
\begin{align*}
\left(|A+B|^p-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma}\right)_+
&\leq
2^{p-1}(|A|^p-\lambda^p(h/2)^{p+\gamma})_+
+2^{p-1}(|B|^p-\lambda^p(h/2)^{p+\gamma})_+\\
&\quad+(2^{-\gamma}-1)\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma}
\mathbf1_{\{|A+B|>\lambda h^{1+\gamma/p}\}}.
\end{align*}
Applying this inequality with $A:=g(x+h/2)-g(x)$ and $B:=g(x+h)-g(x+h/2)$,
we conclude  that, for any $h\in (0,|I|)$,
\begin{align*}
&\int_a^{b-h}
(|g(x+h)-g(x)|^p-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma})_+\,dx\\
&\quad\leq2^{p-1}\int_a^{b-h}(|g(x+h/2)-g(x)|^p
-\lambda^p(h/2)^{p+\gamma})_+\,dx\\
&\qquad\quad+2^{p-1}\int_{a+h/2}^{b-h/2}
(|g(y+h/2)-g(y)|^p-\lambda^p(h/2)^{p+\gamma})_+\,dy\\
&\qquad\quad+(2^{-\gamma}-1)\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma}
\int_a^{b-h}\mathbf1_{\{|g(x+h)-g(x)|>\lambda h^{1+\gamma/p}\}}\,dx\\
&\quad\leq2^p\int_a^{b-h/2}(|g(x+h/2)-g(x)|^p
-\lambda^p(h/2)^{p+\gamma})_+\,dx\\
&\qquad\quad+(2^{-\gamma}-1)\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma}
\int_a^{b-h}\mathbf1_{\{|g(x+h)-g(x)|>\lambda h^{1+\gamma/p}\}}\,dx,
\end{align*}
which further implies that
\begin{align}\label{yishan}
&\int_r^{2r}\int_a^{b-h}\frac{(|g(x+h)-g(x)|^p-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma})_+}{h^{p+1}}\,dx\,dh\notag\\
&\quad\leq\int_{r/2}^r\int_a^{b-s}\frac{(|g(x+s)-g(x)|^p-\lambda^ps^{p+\gamma})_+}{s^{p+1}}\,dx\,ds\notag\\
&\quad\quad+(2^{-\gamma}-1)\lambda^p\int_r^{2r}h^{\gamma-1}\int_a^{b-h}
\mathbf1_{\{|g(x+h)-g(x)|>\lambda h^{1+\gamma/p}\}}\,dx\,dh.
\end{align}
Replacing $r$ with $2^{-k}r$ in \eqref{yishan}, we find that, for any $K\in\mathbb{N}$ and $k\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}$,
\begin{align*}
&\int_{2^{-k}r}^{2^{1-k}r}\int_a^{b-h}
\frac{(|g(x+h)-g(x)|^p-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma})_+}{h^{p+1}}
\,dx\,dh\\
&\quad\le
\int_{2^{-k-1}r}^{2^{-k}r}\int_a^{b-h}
\frac{(|g(x+h)-g(x)|^p-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma})_+}{h^{p+1}}
\,dx\,dh\\
&\qquad +(2^{-\gamma}-1)\lambda^p
\int_{2^{-k}r}^{2^{1-k}r}h^{\gamma-1}
\int_a^{b-h}
\mathbf1_{\{|g(x+h)-g(x)|>\lambda h^{1+\gamma/p}\}}
\,dx\,dh.
\end{align*}
Summing $k$ in the above inequality, we obtain, for any $K\in\mathbb N$,
\begin{align}\label{zhida}
&\int_r^{2r}\int_a^{b-h}\frac{(|g(x+h)-g(x)|^p-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma})_+}{h^{p+1}}
\,dx\,dh\notag\\
&\quad\leq\int_{2^{-K}r}^{2^{1-K}r}\int_a^{b-h}
\frac{(|g(x+h)-g(x)|^p-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma})_+}{h^{p+1}}\,dx\,dh\notag\\
&\quad\quad+(2^{-\gamma}-1)\lambda^p\int_{2^{1-K}r}^{2r}h^{\gamma-1}\int_a^{b-h}
\mathbf1_{\{|g(x+h)-g(x)|>\lambda h^{1+\gamma/p}\}}\,dx\,dh.
\end{align}
Since $1+\gamma/p<0$,
we can  choose $K$ sufficiently large that
$\lambda(2^{1-K}r)^{1+\gamma/p}\geq2\|g\|_{L^\infty(I)}.$
Then, for any $h\in(2^{-K}r,2^{1-K}r)$ and almost every $x\in(a,b-h)$,
\begin{align*}
|g(x+h)-g(x)|\leq2\|g\|_{L^\infty(I)}\leq\lambda(2^{1-K}r)^{1+\frac\gamma p}
\leq\lambda h^{1+\frac\gamma p}
\end{align*}
and hence
\begin{align*}
\int_{2^{-K}r}^{2^{1-K}r}\int_a^{b-h}
\frac{(|g(x+h)-g(x)|^p-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma})_+}{h^{p+1}}
\,dx\,dh=0.
\end{align*}
By this and \eqref{zhida}, we find that
\begin{align*}
&\int_r^{2r}\int_a^{b-h}\frac{(|g(x+h)-g(x)|^p-\lambda^ph^{p+\gamma})_+}{h^{p+1}}\,dx\,dh\\
&\quad\leq(2^{-\gamma}-1)\lambda^p\int_0^{|I|}h^{\gamma-1}\int_a^{b-h}
\mathbf1_{\{|g(x+h)-g(x)|>\lambda h^{1+\gamma/p}\}}\,dx\,dh
=\frac{2^{-\gamma}-1}{2}F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(g;I).
\end{align*}
This proves \eqref{wangji} and hence completes the proof of Lemma \ref{lem-diffbd}.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{prop-1dcoerc}
Let $p\in[1,\infty)$ and $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1]\setminus\{-p\}$.
Let $I\subset\mathbb R$ be a bounded open interval, let
$\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy
$\lambda_j\to0$, and let $\{f_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(I)$ satisfy
$f_j\to f$ in $L^p(I)$ for some $f\in L^p(I)$. Then the following
statements hold.
\begin{itemize}
\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] If $p\in(1,\infty)$, then
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(f_j;I\right)
\geq c_{p,\gamma}\int_I|f'(x)|^p\,dx,
\end{align*}
where the right-hand side is understood to be $\infty$ if
$f\notin W^{1,p}(I)$ and $c_{p,\gamma}$ is a  positive constant depending only on $p$ and $\gamma.$
\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] If $p=1$, then
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(f_j;I\right)
\geq c_\gamma|Df|(I),
\end{align*}
where the right-hand side is understood to be $\infty$ if $f\notin BV(I)$ and $c_{\gamma}$ is a
positive constant depending only on $\gamma.$
\end{itemize}
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Let
\begin{align*}
L:=\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(f_j;I\right).
\end{align*}
If $L=\infty$, there is nothing to prove. We now assume $L<\infty$.
Passing to a subsequence and relabelling, we may assume that
$\lim_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(f_j;I)=L$ and $\lim_{j\to\infty}f_j(x)=f(x)$
for almost every $x\in I.$ Let $p'$ be the conjugate exponent of $p$.
We claim that, for any $\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^\infty(I)$,
\begin{align}\label{yijiceshi}
\left|\int_I f\varphi'\,dx\right|
\lesssim
L^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\varphi\|_{L^{p'}(I)},
\end{align}
where the implicit constant depends only on $p$ and $\gamma$.
If this claim holds, the conclusions of the proposition follow from $L^p$ duality when  $p\in(1,\infty)$
and the structure theorem for BV functions when $p=1$.
We prove \eqref{yijiceshi} by considering two cases.

\textit{Case 1: $-p<\gamma\leq-1$.} In this case, we have  $p\in(1,\infty)$.
Observe  that, if $J\subset I$ is an open interval and $t\in J$ is a
Lebesgue point of $f$, then
\begin{align}\label{kuxiaozhe}
\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_J f\leq f(t)=\lim_{\rho\to 0^+}\frac1{2\rho}
\int_{t-\rho}^{t+\rho}f(y)\,dy\leq\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_J f.
\end{align}
Let $I'\subset I$, and let $0<h<\frac12\mathrm{dist}(I',\partial I)$.
For any $x\in I'$, let $J_{x,h}:=(x-h,x+2h)\subset I.$
Using \eqref{kuxiaozhe} and applying  Lemma \ref{lem-seqosc}, we obtain,
for almost every $x\in I',$
\begin{align}\label{quan}
\left|\frac{f(x+h)-f(x)}h\right|^p
&\leq h^{-p}\left(\operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{J_{x,h}}f-
\operatorname*{ess\,inf}_{J_{x,h}}f\right)^p
\lesssim h^{-1}\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(f_j;J_{x,h}).
\end{align}
On the other hand, by Tonelli's theorem, we find that
\begin{align*}
\int_{I'}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(f_j;J_{x,h})\,dx&=\lambda_j^p\underset{|f_j(y)-f_j(z)|>\lambda_j|y-z|^\beta}
{\int_I\int_I}|y-z|^{\gamma-1}\left(\int_{I'}\mathbf1_{J_{x,h}}(y)
\mathbf1_{J_{x,h}}(z)\,dx\right)\,dz\,dy\\
&\leq3hF_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(f_j;I).
\end{align*}
From this and \eqref{quan}, it follows that
\begin{align*}
\int_{I'}\left|\frac{f(x+h)-f(x)}h\right|^p\,dx&\lesssim
h^{-1}\int_{I'}\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(f_j;J_{x,h})\,dx
\lesssim L,
\end{align*}
which further implies that, for any $\varphi\in C^\infty_{\rm c}(I)$ supported in $I'$,
\begin{align*}
\left|\int_I f\varphi'\,dx\right|^p&=\lim_{h\to 0^+}
\left|\int_{I'}\frac{f(x+h)-f(x)}h\varphi(x)\,dx\right|^p\\
&\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{p'}(I)}^p\varlimsup_{h\to 0^+}
\int_{I'}\left|\frac{f(x+h)-f(x)}h\right|^p\,dx
\lesssim L\|\varphi\|_{L^{p'}(I)}^p.
\end{align*}
This proves \eqref{yijiceshi} in this case.

\textit{Case 2: $\gamma<-p$.}
For any given $M\in (0,\infty)$, let $T_M:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ be the symmetric
truncation defined by setting, for any $t\in\mathbb{R}$,
\begin{align}\label{jieding}
T_M(t):=
\begin{cases}
-M, & t\in(-\infty,-M),\\
t, & t\in[-M,M],\\
M, & t\in(M,\infty).
\end{cases}
\end{align}
Then, for any $s,t\in\mathbb{R},$
\begin{align}\label{deng}
|T_M(t)-T_M(s)|
\leq
|s-t|.
\end{align}
By \eqref{deng}, we find that, for any $j\in\mathbb N$,
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(T_Mf_j;I)&\leq F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(f_j;I)
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
\|T_Mf_j-T_Mf\|_{L^p(I)}\leq\|f_j-f\|_{L^p(I)}\to 0
\end{align*}
as $j\to\infty$. Let  $I:=(a,b)$ and  $r\in(0,\frac{|I|}{2})$.
Recall  that $\lim_{j\to\infty}f_j(x)=f(x)$ for almost every $x\in I.$
From this and \eqref{deng}, we infer that, for almost every $(x,h)\in I\times (0,\infty)$ with
$x,x+h\in I$,
\begin{align*}
\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac{T_Mf_j(x+h)-T_Mf_j(x)}{h}=\frac{T_Mf(x+h)-T_Mf(x)}{h}.
\end{align*}
Using this  and  applying Lemma \ref{lem-diffbd}, we obtain
\begin{align}\label{junchash}
&\int_r^{2r}\int_a^{b-h}\left|\frac{T_Mf(x+h)-T_Mf(x)}h\right|^p\,dx\,\frac{dh}h
\nonumber\\
&\quad\leq\varliminf_{j\to\infty}\int_r^{2r}\int_a^{b-h}
\left|\frac{T_Mf_j(x+h)-T_Mf_j(x)}h\right|^p\,dx\,\frac{dh}h
\nonumber\\
&\quad\leq\varliminf_{j\to\infty}\left[
\frac{2^{-\gamma}-1}{2}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(f_j;I)
+\frac{2^\gamma-1}{\gamma}\lambda_j^p|I|r^\gamma
\right]=\frac{2^{-\gamma}-1}{2}L.
\end{align}
It is obvious that, for any $\varphi\in C^\infty_{\rm c}(I)$,
\begin{align*}
\left|\int_I T_Mf\varphi'\,dx\right|^p
=
\lim_{h\to0^+}
\left|\int_a^{b-h}
\frac{T_Mf(x+h)-T_Mf(x)}h\varphi(x)\,dx\right|^p.
\end{align*}
This, combined with the fact that $\int_r^{2r}\frac{dh}h=\log2$, H\"older's inequality, and
\eqref{junchash}, implies that, for any $\varphi\in C^\infty_{\rm c}(I)$,
\begin{align*}
\left|\int_I T_Mf\varphi'\,dx\right|^p
&=\frac1{\log2}\lim_{r\to0^+}\int_r^{2r}
\left|\int_a^{b-h}
\frac{T_Mf(x+h)-T_Mf(x)}h\varphi(x)\,dx\right|^p\frac{dh}h\\
&\leq\frac{\|\varphi\|_{L^{p'}(I)}^p}{\log2}
\varlimsup_{r\to0^+}\int_r^{2r}\int_a^{b-h}
\left|\frac{T_Mf(x+h)-T_Mf(x)}h\right|^p\,dx\,\frac{dh}h
\lesssim L\|\varphi\|_{L^{p'}(I)}^p.
\end{align*}
Letting $M\to\infty$ in the estimate as above, we conclude that \eqref{yijiceshi} holds in this case.
This completes the proof of Proposition \ref{prop-1dcoerc}.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem-slicing}
Let $N\geq2$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\gamma\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}$, and
$\Omega\subset\mathbb R^N$ be an open set. Then, for any
measurable function $g:\Omega\to\mathbb R$ and any $\lambda\in(0,\infty)$,
\begin{align}\label{qiege}
F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(g;\Omega)
=\frac12\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_{\sigma^\perp}
F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(g_{z,\sigma};\Omega_{z,\sigma})\,dz\,d\sigma.
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Let $\beta:=1+\frac{\gamma}{p}$.
Recall that, for any nonnegative measurable function $v$ on $\Omega$
and any $\sigma\in\mathbb S^{N-1}$,
\begin{align}\label{wangzheni}
\int_\Omega v(x)\,dx=\int_{\sigma^\perp}\int_{\Omega_{z,\sigma}}
v(z+t\sigma)\,dt\,dz.
\end{align}
By this and the polar coordinate decomposition, we obtain
\begin{align}\label{xiangbei}
F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(g;\Omega)&=\lambda^p\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_\Omega
\int_{\{r>0:\,x+r\sigma\in\Omega\}}
\mathbf1_{\left\{|g(x+r\sigma)-g(x)|>\lambda r^\beta\right\}}
r^{\gamma-1}\,dr\,dx\,d\sigma\notag\\
&=\lambda^p\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_{\sigma^\perp}
\int_{\Omega_{z,\sigma}}\int_{\{r>0:\,t+r\in\Omega_{z,\sigma}\}}
\mathbf1_{\{|g_{z,\sigma}(t+r)-g_{z,\sigma}(t)|>\lambda r^\beta\}}
r^{\gamma-1}\,dr\,dt\,dz\,d\sigma\notag\\
&=\lambda^p\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_{\sigma^\perp}
\int_{\Omega_{z,\sigma}}\int_{\Omega_{z,\sigma}\cap(t,\infty)}
\mathbf1_{\left\{|g_{z,\sigma}(s)-g_{z,\sigma}(t)|
>\lambda|s-t|^\beta\right\}}|s-t|^{\gamma-1}\,ds\,dt\,dz\,d\sigma.
\end{align}
On the other hand, it is easy to verify that, for  any $\sigma\in\mathbb S^{N-1}$ and  $z\in\sigma^\perp$,
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(g_{z,\sigma};\Omega_{z,\sigma})=
2\lambda^p\int_{\Omega_{z,\sigma}}\int_{\Omega_{z,\sigma}\cap(t,\infty)}
\mathbf1_{\left\{|g_{z,\sigma}(s)-g_{z,\sigma}(t)|
>\lambda|s-t|^\beta\right\}}|s-t|^{\gamma-1}\,ds\,dt.
\end{align*}
This, together with \eqref{xiangbei}, implies that \eqref{qiege} holds. This
finishes the proof of Lemma \ref{lem-slicing}.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
The case $N=1$ directly follows from Proposition \ref{prop-1dcoerc}.
Assume now that $N\geq2$. Let
\begin{align*}
L:=\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right).
\end{align*}
If $L=\infty$, there is nothing to prove. We now assume $L<\infty$.
Passing to a subsequence and relabelling, we may assume that
\begin{align}\label{kongge}
\lim_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)=L
\ \ \text{and}\ \
\sum_{j=1}^\infty\|u_j-u\|_{L^p(\Omega)}^p<\infty.
\end{align}
We claim that, for any $\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^\infty(\Omega;\mathbb R^N)$,
\begin{align}\label{duoceshi}
\left|\int_\Omega u\operatorname{div}\varphi\,dx\right|
\lesssim L^{1/p}\|\varphi\|_{L^{p'}(\Omega;\mathbb R^N)},
\end{align}
where the implicit constant depends only on $N,p,$ and $\gamma$.
If this claim holds, the conclusions of the proposition follow from
$L^p$ duality when $p\in(1,\infty)$ and the structure theorem for $BV$ functions
when $p=1$. We prove \eqref{duoceshi} by applying the one-dimensional
estimate on almost all line sections of $\Omega$ and integrating over
their positions and directions.

Fix $\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^\infty(\Omega;\mathbb R^N)$.
Note that, for any $\sigma\in\mathbb S^{N-1}$ and $i,k\in\{1,\ldots,N\}$,
\begin{align*}
\partial_\sigma(\varphi\cdot\sigma)
=\sum_{i,k=1}^N\sigma_i\sigma_k\partial_i\varphi_k
\ \ \text{and}\ \
\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\sigma_i\sigma_k\,d\sigma
=\frac{|\mathbb S^{N-1}|}{N}\delta_{ik},
\end{align*}
where  $\delta_{ik}$ denotes the Kronecker delta.
Consequently,
\begin{align*}
\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}
\partial_\sigma(\varphi(x)\cdot\sigma)\,d\sigma
=\sum_{i,k=1}^N\partial_i\varphi_k(x)
\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\sigma_i\sigma_k\,d\sigma
=\frac{|\mathbb S^{N-1}|}{N}\operatorname{div}\varphi(x).
\end{align*}
This,  together with the fact that
\begin{align*}
\frac{d}{dt}\left(\varphi(z+t\sigma)\cdot\sigma\right)
=\partial_\sigma(\varphi\cdot\sigma)(z+t\sigma),
\end{align*}
implies that
\begin{align}\label{fangcesh}
\left|\int_\Omega u\operatorname{div}\varphi\,dx\right|
&=\frac{N}{|\mathbb S^{N-1}|}\left|\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_\Omega
u(x)\partial_\sigma(\varphi\cdot\sigma)(x)\,dx\,d\sigma\right|\notag\\
&=\frac{N}{|\mathbb S^{N-1}|}
\left|\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_{\sigma^\perp}
\int_{\Omega_{z,\sigma}}u(z+t\sigma)
\partial_\sigma(\varphi\cdot\sigma)(z+t\sigma)
\,dt\,dz\,d\sigma\right|\notag\\
&\leq\frac{N}{|\mathbb S^{N-1}|}
\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_{\sigma^\perp}
\left|\int_{\Omega_{z,\sigma}}u_{z,\sigma}(t)
\frac{d}{dt}\left(\varphi(z+t\sigma)\cdot\sigma\right)
\,dt\right|\,dz\,d\sigma.
\end{align}

Next, we show that, for
almost every $(\sigma, z)\in (\mathbb S^{N-1}, \sigma^{\perp}) $ and any
$\psi\in C_{\rm c}^\infty(\Omega_{z,\sigma})$,
\begin{align}\label{qieceshi}
\left|\int_{\Omega_{z,\sigma}}u_{z,\sigma}\psi'\,dt\right|
\lesssim
\left[\varliminf_{j\to\infty}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left((u_j)_{z,\sigma};\Omega_{z,\sigma}\right)\right]^{\frac1p}
\|\psi\|_{L^{p'}(\Omega_{z,\sigma})}.
\end{align}
Assume for the moment that  \eqref{qieceshi} holds.
By \eqref{qieceshi}, \eqref{fangcesh}, and H\"older's inequality,
we conclude that
\begin{align}\label{moment}
\left|\int_\Omega u\operatorname{div}\varphi\,dx\right|&\lesssim
\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_{\sigma^\perp}
\left[\varliminf_{j\to\infty}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left((u_j)_{z,\sigma};\Omega_{z,\sigma}\right)\right]^{\frac1p}
\|\varphi(z+\cdot\,\sigma)\cdot\sigma\|_{L^{p'}(\Omega_{z,\sigma})}
\,dz\,d\sigma\notag\\
&\lesssim\left[\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_{\sigma^\perp}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left((u_j)_{z,\sigma};\Omega_{z,\sigma}\right)
\,dz\,d\sigma\right]^{\frac1p}\notag\\
&\quad\times\left[\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_{\sigma^\perp}
\|\varphi(z+\cdot\,\sigma)\cdot\sigma\|_{L^{p'}(\Omega_{z,\sigma})}^{p'}
\,dz\,d\sigma\right]^{\frac1{p'}}.
\end{align}
Furthermore, from \eqref{wangzheni}, we deduce that
\begin{align}\label{houzhi}
\left(\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_{\sigma^\perp}
\|\varphi(z+\cdot\,\sigma)\cdot\sigma\|_{L^{p'}(\Omega_{z,\sigma})}^{p'}
\,dz\,d\sigma\right)^{\frac1{p'}}&=\left(\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_\Omega
|\varphi(x)\cdot\sigma|^{p'}\,dx\,d\sigma\right)^{\frac1{p'}}\notag\\
&\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{p'}(\Omega;\mathbb R^N)}.
\end{align}
Using Fatou's lemma and \eqref{qiege}, we conclude that
\begin{align*}
\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_{\sigma^\perp}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left((u_j)_{z,\sigma};\Omega_{z,\sigma}\right)\,dz\,d\sigma
&\leq\varliminf_{j\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_{\sigma^\perp}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left((u_j)_{z,\sigma};\Omega_{z,\sigma}\right)\,dz\,d\sigma\nonumber\\
&=2\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)=2L.
\end{align*}
From this, \eqref{houzhi}, and \eqref{moment}, we infer that
\eqref{duoceshi} holds.

Now, it remains to prove \eqref{qieceshi}.
By \eqref{wangzheni} and \eqref{kongge}, we obtain
\begin{align*}
\int_{\mathbb S^{N-1}}\int_{\sigma^\perp}
\sum_{j=1}^\infty\left\|(u_j)_{z,\sigma}-u_{z,\sigma}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega_{z,\sigma})}^p
\,dz\,d\sigma=\left|\mathbb S^{N-1}\right|\sum_{j=1}^\infty
\|u_j-u\|_{L^p(\Omega)}^p<\infty.
\end{align*}
Consequently, for almost every $(\sigma,z)\in \mathbb{S}^{N-1}\times \sigma^\perp$,
$(u_j)_{z,\sigma}\to u_{z,\sigma}$ in $L^p(\Omega_{z,\sigma})$ as $j\to\infty$.
Since $\Omega$ is a bounded open set,
it follows that $\Omega_{z,\sigma}$
is a bounded open subset of $\mathbb R$. Thus, $\Omega_{z,\sigma}=\bigcup_k I_k,$
where $\{I_k\}_k$ is a sequence of disjoint bounded open intervals.
This, combined with \eqref{yijiceshi} and H\"older's inequality, implies that
\begin{align*}
\left|\int_{\Omega_{z,\sigma}}u_{z,\sigma}\psi'\,dt\right|
&\leq\sum_k\left|\int_{I_k}u_{z,\sigma}\psi'\,dt\right|\lesssim\sum_k\left[\varliminf_{j\to\infty}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left((u_j)_{z,\sigma};I_k\right)\right]^{\frac1p}\|\psi\|_{L^{p'}(I_k)}\\
&\lesssim\left[\sum_k\varliminf_{j\to\infty}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left((u_j)_{z,\sigma};I_k\right)\right]^{\frac1p}
\|\psi\|_{L^{p'}(\Omega_{z,\sigma})}\\
&\lesssim\left[\varliminf_{j\to\infty}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left((u_j)_{z,\sigma};\Omega_{z,\sigma}\right)\right]^{\frac1p}
\|\psi\|_{L^{p'}(\Omega_{z,\sigma})}.
\end{align*}
This proves \eqref{qieceshi}, which completes the proof of Proposition \ref{prop-coerc}.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem-scaling}
Let  $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\gamma\in\mathbb R\setminus\{0\}$,
$U\subset\mathbb R^N$ be an open set, and $w:U\to\mathbb R$ be a measurable
function. Let $x_0\in\mathbb R^N$, $R\in\mathcal O(N)$,
$r\in(0,\infty)$, $a\in\mathbb R\setminus\{0\}$, and $b\in\mathbb R$.
Define the function $u:x_0+rRU\to\mathbb R$ by setting, for any $z\in U$,
$u(x_0+rRz):=arw(z)+b$. Then, for any $\lambda\in(0,\infty)$,
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(u;x_0+rRU)
=|a|^pr^NF_{\frac{\lambda r^{\gamma/p}}{|a|},p,\gamma}(w;U).
\end{align*}
Consequently,
\begin{align}\label{bisuo}
m_{p,\gamma}(aw+b)=|a|^pm_{p,\gamma}(w).
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem-mplsc}
Let  $p\in[1,\infty)$ and
$\gamma\in\mathbb R\setminus\{0\}$. The map
$m_{p,\gamma}:L^p(Q_0)\to[0,\infty]$ is lower semicontinuous. More precisely,
let $w\in L^p(Q_0)$, and let
$\{w_n\}_{n\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(Q_0)$ satisfy
$w_n\to w$ in $L^p(Q_0)$. Then
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{n\to\infty}m_{p,\gamma}(w_n)\geq m_{p,\gamma}(w).
\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem-cellbds}
Let  $p\in[1,\infty)$ and
$\gamma\in\mathbb R\setminus\{0\}$. Then the following assertions hold.
\begin{itemize}
\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] If $\gamma\in(0,\infty)$, then
\begin{align*}
\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\ell;Q_0)=\frac{K_{N,p}}{\gamma}.
\end{align*}
If $\gamma\in(-\infty,0)$, then
\begin{align*}
\lim_{\lambda\to0^+}F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\ell;Q_0)=\frac{K_{N,p}}{|\gamma|}.
\end{align*}
Consequently, $0\leq C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}\leq\frac{K_{N,p}}{|\gamma|}.$

\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] There exists a sequence
$\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfying
$\lambda_j\to\infty$ if $\gamma\in(0,\infty)$ and $\lambda_j\to0$ if $\gamma\in(-\infty,0)$.
For this sequence, there exists a sequence
$\{v_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(Q_0)$ satisfying
$v_j\to\ell$ in $L^p(Q_0)$ and
\begin{align*}
\lim_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(v_j;Q_0)
=C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}.
\end{align*}
\end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Let
\begin{align*}
L:=\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)\in[0,\infty].
\end{align*}
If $L=\infty$, then the desired conclusion holds automatically. We now assume $L<\infty$.
By Proposition \ref{prop-coerc}, we conclude that
$u\in W^{1,p}(\Omega)$. Define the measure $d\mu(x):=|\nabla u(x)|^p\,dx$
on $\Omega.$ Without loss of generality, we may assume  $\mu(\Omega)>0.$
Let
\begin{align*}
A_0:=\left\{x\in\Omega:\nabla u(x)\neq0,\ \lim_{\rho\to0^+}
\fint_{B(x,\rho)}|\nabla u(y)-\nabla u(x)|^p\,dy=0\right\}.
\end{align*}
From the Lebesgue differentiation theorem, we deduce that $\mu(\Omega\setminus A_0)=0$.
Let $x\in A_0$ and $\xi:=\nabla u(x).$ Choose $R_x\in\mathcal O(N)$ such that $ R_xe_N=\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}$ and
choose $ r_x\in(0,\infty)$ such that, for any $r\in(0, r_x)$,  $Q_r^{R_x}(x)\subset\Omega$. For any $r\in(0, r_x)$,
$j\in\mathbb{N}$, and $z\in Q_0$,
define
\begin{align*}
b_{x,r}:=\fint_{Q_r^{R_x}(x)}u(y)\,dy
\ \ \text{and}\ \
u_{x,r}(z):=\frac{u(x+rR_xz)-b_{x,r}}{|\xi|r}.
\end{align*}
Then  $u_j\to u$ in $L^p(\Omega)$ as $j\to\infty$.
Using Lemma \ref{lem-cellbds}, we find that $C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}<\infty$.
By  this, Lemmas \ref{lem-scaling} and \ref{lem-mplsc}, and
an argument similar to that used in the proof of \cite[(4.6)]{lyyyz26}, we conclude that
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j;Q_r^{R_x}(x)\right)
\geq|\xi|^pr^Nm_{p,\gamma}(u_{x,r})\geq\frac{C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}-\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}
\mu\left(Q_r^{R_x}(x)\right).
\end{align*}
Using this and a Morse type covering argument similar to that used in the proof of \cite[Theorem 4.1]{lyyyz26},
we find that \eqref{xiaji} holds. This completes the proof of Theorem \ref{thm-pliminf}.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{prop-profend}
Let $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1)$ and $q\in BV(I_0)$.  Assume that $q$ is nondecreasing.  Then
\begin{align}\label{fuduan}
 m_{1,\gamma}(W_q)\geq C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}|Dq|(I_0),
\end{align}
where $I_0:=(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})$ and, for any  $z\in Q_0,$ $W_q(z):=q(z_N).$
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem-locbvneg}
Let $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1)$, $v\in BV(\mathbb R^N)$, and $E\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded Borel set.  Then
there exists a  positive constant $C_{N,\gamma}$, depending only on $N$ and $\gamma$, such that, for any $r,\lambda\in (0,\infty)$,
\begin{align*}
\lambda
\underset{|v(x)-v(y)|>\lambda|x-y|^{\gamma+1}}
{\int_E\int_{B(x,r)}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx
\leq C_{N,\gamma}|Dv|(E^{(3r)}).
\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem-collar}
Let $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1)$ and $q\in BV(I_0)$ be nondecreasing. Assume that  $q(-\frac{1}{2}+)=0$
and $q(\frac{1}{2}-)=1$, where $q(-\frac{1}{2}+):=
\lim_{t\to -\frac{1}{2}^+}q(t)$ and $q(\frac{1}{2}-):=\lim_{t\to\frac{1}{2}^-}q(t).$
Then
\begin{align}\label{lingbian}
\lim_{\rho\to 0^+}\varlimsup_{\lambda\to0^+}
\lambda
\underset{|W_q(x)-W_q(y)|>\lambda|x-y|^{\gamma+1}}
{\int_{B_\rho}\int_{Q_0}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx=0.
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Extend $q$ constantly outside $I_0$ by setting, for any $t\in\mathbb R$,
\begin{align*}
\widetilde q(t):=\begin{cases}
0,&\displaystyle t\in\left(-\infty,-\frac{1}{2}\right],\\
q(t),&\displaystyle t\in\left(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}\right),\\
1,&\displaystyle t\in\left[\frac{1}{2},\infty\right).
\end{cases}
\end{align*}
Then $\widetilde{q}$ is continuous at $-\frac{1}{2}$ and $\frac{1}{2}$, which implies that
\begin{align*}
D\widetilde q\left(\left\{-\frac{1}{2}\right\}\right)
=D\widetilde q\left(\left\{\frac{1}{2}\right\}\right)=0.
\end{align*}
Choose $\theta\in C_{\rm c}^\infty(\mathbb R)$ such that $\theta\equiv 1$   on a
neighborhood of $[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}].$ If $N=1$, let $\zeta\equiv1$ and, if $N\ge2$,
choose $\zeta\in C_{\rm c}^\infty(\mathbb R^{N-1})$ such that $\zeta\equiv 1$   on a neighborhood
of $[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{N-1}.$ For any $z:=(z',z_N)\in\mathbb{R}^N,$ define
\begin{align*}
\widetilde W_q(z',z_N):=\zeta(z')\theta(z_N)\widetilde q(z_N).
\end{align*}
Then, as is showed in \cite[(4.43)]{lyyyz26}, we find that
$\widetilde W_q\in BV(\mathbb R^N)$ and
\begin{align}\label{bianling}
\left|D\widetilde W_q\right|(\partial Q_0)=0.
\end{align}
Applying Lemma \ref{lem-locbvneg} and using polar coordinates, we
conclude that, for any $\rho\in(0,\frac{1}{20})$ and $\lambda\in(0,\infty)$,
\begin{align*}
&\lambda
\underset{|W_q(x)-W_q(y)|>\lambda|x-y|^{\gamma+1}}
{\int_{B_\rho}\int_{Q_0}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx\\
&\quad\leq\lambda
\underset{|\widetilde W_q(x)-\widetilde W_q(y)|>\lambda|x-y|^{\gamma+1}}
{\int_{B_\rho}\int_{B(x,\rho)}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx
+\lambda\int_{B_\rho}\int_{\mathbb R^N\setminus B(x,\rho)}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx\\
&\quad\lesssim\left|D\widetilde W_q\right|\left(B_\rho^{(3\rho)}\right)
+\lambda\rho^\gamma|B_\rho|.
\end{align*}
Since
\begin{align*}
B_\rho^{(3\rho)}\subset
\left\{x\in\mathbb R^N:\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial Q_0)<4\rho\right\},
\end{align*}
letting first $\lambda\to0^+$ and then $\rho\to0^+$ and using \eqref{bianling}, we find that
\eqref{lingbian} holds. This completes the proof of Lemma \ref{lem-collar}.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem-cutoff}
Let $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\gamma\in(-\infty,0)$, and $\lambda\in(0,\infty)$. Then the following statements hold.
\begin{itemize}
\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] For any given measurable sets $E, D\subset\mathbb R^N$,
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\ell;E,D)\leq\frac{K_{N,p}}{|\gamma|}|E|.
\end{align*}
Consequently, $F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\ell;E)\leq\frac{K_{N,p}}{|\gamma|}|E|$.
\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] Let $U\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded open set,
$u,v\in L^p(U)$, and $\eta:U\to[0,1]$ be Lipschitz.
Then, for any $\alpha\in(0,1)$,
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(w;U)\leq\frac{F_{\alpha\lambda,p,\gamma}(u;U)}{\alpha^p}
+\frac{F_{\alpha\lambda,p,\gamma}(v;E_\eta,U)}{\alpha^p}
+\frac{\mathcal H^{N-1}\left(\mathbb S^{N-1}\right)L_\eta^p\left\|u-v\right\|_{L^p(U)}^p}
{|\gamma|(1-\alpha)^p},
\end{align*}
where $w:=\eta u+(1-\eta)v$, $E_\eta:=\{x\in U:\eta(x)<1\}$, and
\begin{align*}
L_\eta:=\sup_{\genfrac{}{}{0pt}{}{x,y\in U}{x\neq y}}
\frac{|\eta(x)-\eta(y)|}{|x-y|}<\infty.
\end{align*}
\end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem-nearmin}
Let $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1)$ and $q\in BV(I_0)$ be nondecreasing with
$q(-\frac12+)=0$ and $q(\frac12-)=1$. Then, for any $\varepsilon\in(0,\infty)$,
there exist $\rho\in(0,\frac1{20})$ and sequences
$\{\widehat\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ and
$\{\widehat v_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^\infty(Q_0)$ such that
the following statements hold.
\begin{itemize}
\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] For any $j\in\mathbb N$, $0\leq\widehat v_j\leq1$ and
\begin{align}\label{jinbian}
\widehat v_j=W_q\quad\text{on }\left\{z\in Q_0:
\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)\leq\rho\right\}.
\end{align}
Moreover, $\lim_{j\to\infty}\widehat v_j=W_q$ in $L^1(Q_0)$.

\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] $\lim_{j\to\infty}\widehat\lambda_j=0$.

\item[$\mathrm{(iii)}$] It holds that
\begin{align}\label{jineng}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\widehat\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(\widehat v_j;Q_0\right)
\leq m_{1,\gamma}(W_q)+\varepsilon.
\end{align}
\end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
If $m_{1,\gamma}(W_q)=\infty$, take $\rho:=\frac1{40}$,
$\widehat\lambda_j:=j^{-1}$, and $\widehat v_j:=W_q$.
We therefore assume that $m_{1,\gamma}(W_q)<\infty$. Define $P:\mathbb R\to[0,1]$ by
setting, for any $s\in\mathbb{R}$,
\begin{align*}
P(s):=\begin{cases}
0, & s\in(-\infty,0],\\
s, & s\in(0,1),\\
1, & s\in[1,\infty).
\end{cases}
\end{align*}
Then $P$ is $1$-Lipschitz and $P\circ W_q=W_q$ almost everywhere in $Q_0$.
By the definition of $m_{1,\gamma}(W_q)$, we find that there exist sequences
$\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ and
$\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^1(Q_0)$ such that
$\lambda_j\to0$, $u_j\to W_q$ in $L^1(Q_0)$, and
\begin{align}\label{sishi}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(u_j;Q_0\right)
\leq m_{1,\gamma}(W_q)+\frac\varepsilon2.
\end{align}
Let $j\in\mathbb{N}$ and $v_j:=P\circ u_j$.
Then $v_j\in L^\infty(Q_0)$, $0\leq v_j\leq1$ almost everywhere in $Q_0$, and
\begin{align*}
\left\|v_j-W_q\right\|_{L^1(Q_0)}=\left\|P\circ u_j-P\circ W_q\right\|_{L^1(Q_0)}
\leq\left\|u_j-W_q\right\|_{L^1(Q_0)}\to0
\end{align*}
as $j\to\infty$. Moreover, from \eqref{sishi}, we deduce that
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(v_j;Q_0\right)
\leq\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(u_j;Q_0\right)
\leq m_{1,\gamma}(W_q)+\frac\varepsilon2.
\end{align*}
Using this and passing  to a subsequence,
we may assume that
$\lambda_j\to0$, $v_j\to W_q$ in $L^1(Q_0)$,
$0\leq v_j\leq1$ almost everywhere in $Q_0$ for any $j\in\mathbb N$, and
\begin{align}\label{hehu}
\lim_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(v_j;Q_0\right)
\leq m_{1,\gamma}(W_q)+\frac\varepsilon2.
\end{align}
By Lemma~\ref{lem-collar}, we conclude that there exists
$\rho\in(0,\frac1{20})$ such that
\begin{align}\label{jinceng}
\varlimsup_{\lambda\to0^+}
F_{\lambda,1,\gamma}\left(W_q;B_{2\rho},Q_0\right)
=\varlimsup_{\lambda\to0^+}
\lambda
\underset{|W_q(x)-W_q(y)|>\lambda|x-y|^{\gamma+1}}
{\int_{B_{2\rho}}\int_{Q_0}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx
\leq\frac\varepsilon2.
\end{align}
Let
\begin{align*}
B_{2\rho}:=\left\{z\in Q_0:
\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)<2\rho\right\}.
\end{align*}
Choose a Lipschitz function $\chi_\rho:Q_0\to[0,1]$ such that
$0\leq\chi_\rho\leq1$, $\chi_\rho(z)=0$ when
$\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)\leq\rho$, $\chi_\rho(z)=1$ when
$\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)\geq2\rho$.
For any $j\in\mathbb N$, define
\begin{align*}
\widehat v_j&:=\chi_\rho v_j+(1-\chi_\rho)W_q.
\end{align*}
Then $0\leq\widehat v_j\leq1$ and
\begin{align*}
\left\|\widehat v_j-W_q\right\|_{L^1(Q_0)}\leq\left\|v_j-W_q\right\|_{L^1(Q_0)}\to0
\end{align*}
as $j\to\infty.$ Choose a sequence $\{\eta_j\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset (0,\infty)$
such that
\begin{align}\label{qingjie}
\lim_{j\to\infty}\eta_j=0\ \ \text{and}\ \
\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac{\|v_j-W_q\|_{L^1(Q_0)}}{\eta_j}=0.
\end{align}
Define $\widehat\lambda_j:=(1+\eta_j)\lambda_j.$ Applying Lemma~\ref{lem-cutoff}(ii), we obtain
\begin{align*}
F_{\widehat\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(\widehat v_j;Q_0\right)
&\leq(1+\eta_j)F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}(v_j;Q_0)+(1+\eta_j)F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(W_q;B_{2\rho},Q_0\right)\notag\\
&\quad+\frac{\mathcal H^{N-1}(\mathbb S^{N-1})L_{\chi_\rho}(1+\eta_j)}{|\gamma|\eta_j}\|v_j-W_q\|_{L^1(Q_0)},
\end{align*}
where $L_{\chi_\rho}<\infty$ is the Lipschitz constant of $\chi_\rho$.
Letting $j\to\infty$ in the above inequality and using \eqref{jinceng}, \eqref{qingjie},
and \eqref{hehu}, we conclude that
\begin{align*}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\widehat\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(\widehat v_j;Q_0\right)
\leq
\lim_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(v_j;Q_0\right)+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
\leq m_{1,\gamma}(W_q)+\varepsilon.
\end{align*}
This shows \eqref{jineng},
which completes the proof of Lemma \ref{lem-nearmin}.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem-skeleton}
Let $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1)$, $L\in\mathbb N$, and $\mathscr Q:=\{Q_1,\ldots,Q_L\}$ be a
finite family of pairwise disjoint open cubes contained in $Q_0$
and define
\begin{align*}
S_{\mathscr Q}:=\overline{Q_0\setminus\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^LQ_i\right)}.
\end{align*}
Let $ U\in BV(Q_0)\cap L^\infty(Q_0)$ and assume that $U$ admits an extension
$\widetilde U\in BV(\mathbb R^N)\cap L^\infty(\mathbb R^N)$
such that $\widetilde U=U$ almost everywhere in $Q_0$ and
\begin{align}\label{geling}
|D\widetilde U|(S_{\mathscr Q})=0.
\end{align}
For $\lambda\in(0,\infty)$, define
\begin{align*}
 F_{\lambda,1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}(U;\mathscr Q):=\lambda\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0pt}{}{1\leq i,j\leq L}{i\neq j}}
\underset{|U(x)-U(y)|>\lambda|x-y|^{\gamma+1}}
{\int_{Q_i}\int_{Q_j}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx.
\end{align*}
Then
\begin{align}\label{gexiao}
\lim_{\lambda\to0^+}F_{\lambda,1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}(U;\mathscr Q)=0.
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Without loss of generality, we may assume that, for any $x\in Q_0,$ $|U(x)|\leq \|U\|_{L^\infty(Q_0)}$.
If $\|U\|_{L^\infty(Q_0)}=0$, then \eqref{gexiao} holds automatically.
Assume now that $\|U\|_{L^\infty(Q_0)}>0$. Let $R_\lambda\in (0,\infty)$ be determined below and
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda,1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}(U;\mathscr Q)=I_\lambda^{\rm near}+I_\lambda^{\rm far},
\end{align*}
where the domains of integration in $I_\lambda^{\rm near}$ and
$I_\lambda^{\rm far}$ are restricted to $|x-y|<R_\lambda$ and
$|x-y|\geq R_\lambda$, respectively. Then
\begin{align}\label{hua}
I_\lambda^{\rm far}\leq\lambda\underset{|x-y|\geq R_\lambda}{\int_{Q_0}\int_{Q_0}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx\leq\lambda|Q_0|\int_{|z|\geq R_\lambda}|z|^{\gamma-N}\,dz
\lesssim\lambda |Q_0|R_\lambda^\gamma.
\end{align}
Let $i,j\in\{1,\ldots,L\}$ with $i\neq j$, and fix points $x\in Q_i$ and $y\in Q_j$ such that
$|x-y|<R_\lambda$.
Since $Q_i$ and $Q_j$ are disjoint, it follows that there exists a point $z\in[x,y]\cap S_{\mathscr Q}$,
which implies that
\begin{align*}
\operatorname{dist}(x,S_{\mathscr Q})\leq |x-z|\leq |x-y|<R_\lambda.
\end{align*}
Consequently, $x\in S_{\mathscr Q}^{(R_\lambda)}$ and $y\in B(x,R_\lambda)$.
Using this and applying Lemma~\ref{lem-locbvneg} with $E:=S_{\mathscr Q}^{(R_\lambda)}$
and $r:=R_\lambda$, we conclude that
\begin{align*}
I_\lambda^{\rm near}\leq\lambda
\underset{|\widetilde U(x)-\widetilde U(y)|>\lambda|x-y|^{\gamma+1}}
{\int_{S_{\mathscr Q}^{(R_\lambda)}}\int_{B(x,R_\lambda)}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx\lesssim\left|D\widetilde U\right|\left(S_{\mathscr Q}^{(4R_\lambda)}\right).
\end{align*}
From this and \eqref{hua}, we infer that
\begin{align}\label{kunao}
F_{\lambda,1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}(U;\mathscr Q)
\lesssim\left|D\widetilde U\right|\left(S_{\mathscr Q}^{(4R_\lambda)}\right)
+\lambda R_\lambda^\gamma|Q_0|.
\end{align}
Now, let  $R_\lambda:=\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2\gamma}}$. Then
\begin{align*}
\lim_{\lambda\to 0^+}R_\lambda=0
\quad\text{and}\quad
\lim_{\lambda\to 0^+}\lambda R_\lambda^\gamma=\lim_{\lambda\to 0^+}\lambda^{\frac12}=0.
\end{align*}
Letting $\lambda\to0^+$
in \eqref{kunao}
and using \eqref{geling}, we find that \eqref{gexiao}
holds. This completes the proof of Lemma \ref{lem-skeleton}.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Since $q$  is nondecreasing, it follows that $|Dq|(I_0)=q(\frac{1}{2}-)-q(-\frac{1}{2}+)$, where
$q(-\frac{1}{2}+):=\lim_{t\to -\frac{1}{2}^+}q(t)$ and $q(\frac{1}{2}-):=\lim_{t\to\frac{1}{2}^-}q(t).$
If $|Dq|(I_0)=0,$ then \eqref{fuduan} holds  automatically. Let $|Dq|(I_0)\in (0,\infty).$ For any $t\in I_0,$ define
\begin{align*}
\widetilde q(t):=\frac{q(t)-q(-\frac{1}{2}+)}{|Dq|(I_0)}.
\end{align*}
Then $0\leq\widetilde q\leq1$ on $I_0$, $\widetilde q(-\frac{1}{2}+)=0$, and $\widetilde q(\frac{1}{2}-)=1.$
By \eqref{bisuo}, we find that
\begin{align*}
 m_{1,\gamma}(W_q)=|Dq|(I_0)m_{1,\gamma}(W_{\widetilde q}).
\end{align*}
Thus, to prove \eqref{fuduan}, it suffices to show that
\begin{align}\label{poumu}
 m_{1,\gamma}(W_{\widetilde q})\geq C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}.
\end{align}
Fix $m\in\mathbb N$.  For any $k\in\{0,\ldots,m\}$,  let $a_k:=-\frac12+\frac{k}{m}.$
Define the function $q_m:I_0\to\mathbb R$ as follows.  For any
$k\in\{0,\ldots,m-1\}$ and   $t\in(a_k,a_{k+1})$, let
\begin{align*}
q_m(t):=a_k+\frac{1}{m}\widetilde q\left(m[t-a_k]-\frac12\right).
\end{align*}
Moreover, for any $k\in\{1,\ldots, m-1\},$ let $q_m(a_k):=a_k.$ Then we have, for any
$k\in\left\{0,\ldots,m-1\right\}$, $q_m(a_k+)=a_k$ and $q_m(a_{k+1}-)=a_k+\frac{1}{m}=a_{k+1}$,
which implies that, for any $k\in\{1,\ldots,m-1\},$
$Dq_m\left(\left\{a_k\right\}\right)=0$ and
\begin{align*}
|Dq_m|\left((a_k,a_{k+1})\right)=q_m(a_{k+1}-)-q_m(a_k+)=\frac{1}{m}.
\end{align*}
Consequently,
\begin{align*}
|Dq_m|(I_0)=\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}|Dq_m|\left(a_k,a_{k+1}\right)=1.
\end{align*}
Furthermore, for any $k\in \{0,\ldots,m-1\}$ and $t\in(a_k,a_{k+1})$,
\begin{align*}
|q_m(t)-t|=\frac{1}{m}\left|\widetilde q\left(m[t-a_k]-\frac12\right)-m(t-a_k)
\right|\leq\frac{1}{m},
\end{align*}
where we used the facts that $0\leq\widetilde{q}\leq 1$ and $m(t-a_k)\in (0,1)$ in the  last step.
This implies that
\begin{align*}
\sup_{t\in I_0}|q_m(t)-t|\leq\frac{1}{m},
\end{align*}
and hence $W_{q_m}\to \ell$ in $L^1(Q_0)$ as $m\to\infty$. From this and Lemma \ref{lem-mplsc}, we infer that
\begin{align*}
C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}=m_{1,\gamma}(\ell)\leq\varliminf_{m\to\infty}m_{1,\gamma}(W_{q_m}).
\end{align*}
Using this, we conclude that, to prove \eqref{poumu}, it suffices to show that, for any $m\in\mathbb{N},$
\begin{align}\label{zibijiao}
m_{1,\gamma}(W_{q_m})\leq m_{1,\gamma}(W_{\widetilde q}).
\end{align}
Let $\varepsilon\in (0,\infty)$.
Applying Lemma~\ref{lem-nearmin} with $q:=\widetilde q$, we
conclude that there exist
$\rho\in(0,\frac{1}{20})$ and sequences
$\{\widehat\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ and
$\{\widehat v_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^\infty(Q_0)$
having the properties stated in Lemma~\ref{lem-nearmin}. In particular,
$\widehat\lambda_j\to0$, $\widehat v_j\to W_{\widetilde q}$ in $L^1(Q_0)$ as $j\to\infty$, and
\begin{align}\label{pouxiufu}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\widehat\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(\widehat v_j;Q_0\right)
\leq m_{1,\gamma}(W_{\widetilde q})+\varepsilon.
\end{align}

Now, we claim that there exist sequences $\{\Lambda^{(m)}_j\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset (0,\infty)$ and
$\{V_j^{(m)}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset L^1(Q_0)$ such that $\lim_{j\to\infty}\Lambda^{(m)}_j=0$,
$\lim_{j\to\infty}V_j^{(m)}=W_{q_m}$ in $L^1(Q_0)$, and
\begin{align}\label{poushen}
m_{1,\gamma}(W_{q_m})\leq\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}\left(V_j^{(m)};Q_0\right)
\leq m_{1,\gamma}(W_{\widetilde q})+\varepsilon.
\end{align}
If this claim holds, then, letting $\varepsilon\to0^+$ in \eqref{poushen},
we conclude that \eqref{zibijiao} holds, which completes the proof of the present proposition.

Next, we prove the above claim. Note that the intervals $(a_0,a_1),(a_1,a_2),\ldots,(a_{m-1},a_m)$
are pairwise disjoint and cover $I_0$ except for the partition points.
Taking their Cartesian products in the $N$ coordinate directions
gives $m^N$ pairwise disjoint open cubes that cover $Q_0$ except for
their boundaries. More precisely, let
$\mathcal A_m:=\left\{0,\ldots,m-1\right\}^N.$
For any $\alpha=(\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_N)\in\mathcal A_m$, define
\begin{align*}
Q_\alpha:=\prod_{i=1}^N(a_{\alpha_i},a_{\alpha_i+1}).
\end{align*}
Then the family $\mathscr Q_m:=\left\{Q_\alpha:\alpha\in\mathcal A_m\right\}$
consists of pairwise disjoint open cubes and covers $Q_0$ except for
their boundaries.  Furthermore, for any $\alpha \in\mathcal{A}_m$, the center of $Q_\alpha$ is
\begin{align*}
c_\alpha&:=\left(\frac{a_{\alpha_1}+a_{\alpha_1+1}}{2},\ldots,\frac{a_{\alpha_N}+a_{\alpha_N+1}}{2}\right)
=-\frac12(1,\ldots,1)+\frac{1}{m}\left(\alpha_1+\frac12,\ldots,\alpha_N+\frac12\right),
\end{align*}
and hence $Q_\alpha=c_\alpha+\frac{1}{m}Q_0.$
From the definitions of $W_{q_m}$ and $q_m$, it follows that, for any $\alpha\in \mathcal{A}_m$
with $k:=\alpha_N$ and for any $x\in Q_\alpha$,
\begin{align}\label{poujuti}
W_{q_m}(x)&=q_m(x_N)=a_k+\frac{1}{m}\widetilde q\left(m[x_N-a_k]-\frac12\right)
=a_k+\frac{1}{m}\widetilde{q}\left(m\left[x_N-(c_\alpha)_N\right]\right)\notag\\
&=a_k+\frac{1}{m}\widetilde{q}\left([m(x-c_\alpha)]_N\right)
=a_k+\frac{1}{m}W_{\widetilde{q}}(m(x-c_\alpha)).
\end{align}
Let $j\in\mathbb{N}$. Replacing $W_{\widetilde q}$ in \eqref{poujuti} by $\widehat{v}_j$,
let
\begin{align*}
V_j^{(m)}(x)
:=\begin{cases}
\displaystyle
a_{\alpha_N}+\frac{1}{m}
\widehat v_j\left(m(x-c_\alpha)\right),
&x\in Q_\alpha\text{ for some }\alpha\in\mathcal A_m,\\[2mm]
W_{q_m}(x),&\displaystyle x\in Q_0\setminus
\left(\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_m}Q_\alpha\right).
 \end{cases}
\end{align*}
By a change of variables, we find that
\begin{align*}
\left\|V_j^{(m)}-W_{q_m}\right\|_{L^1(Q_0)}=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_m}
\int_{Q_\alpha}\left|V_j^{(m)}-W_{q_m}\right|\,dx=\frac{1}{m}
\left\|\widehat v_j-W_{\widetilde q}\right\|_{L^1(Q_0)}.
\end{align*}
Thus, $\lim_{j\to\infty}V_j^{(m)}=W_{q_m}$ in $L^1(Q_0)$. Define
$\Lambda_j^{(m)}:=m^\gamma\widehat\lambda_j.$
From Lemma~\ref{lem-scaling}, we infer that, for any $\alpha\in\mathcal A_m$,
\begin{align*}
F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}\left(V_j^{(m)};Q_\alpha\right)=\frac{1}{m^N}
F_{\widehat\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(\widehat v_j;Q_0\right).
\end{align*}
Consequently,
\begin{align}\label{baohe}
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_m}F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}\left(V_j^{(m)};Q_\alpha\right)
=F_{\widehat\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(\widehat v_j;Q_0\right).
\end{align}
Let
\begin{align*}
F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}\left(V_j^{(m)};\mathscr Q_m\right)
&:=\Lambda_j^{(m)}\sum_{\substack{\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal A_m\\\alpha\neq\beta}}
\underset{\left|V_j^{(m)}(x)-V_j^{(m)}(y)\right|
>\Lambda_j^{(m)}|x-y|^{\gamma+1}}
{\int_{Q_\alpha}\int_{Q_\beta}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx.
\end{align*}
By \eqref{baohe}, we conclude that
\begin{align*}
F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}\left(V_j^{(m)};Q_0\right)&\leq\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_m}
F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}\left(V_j^{(m)};Q_\alpha\right)+ F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}
\left(V_j^{(m)};\mathscr Q_m\right)\notag\\
&=F_{\widehat\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(\widehat v_j;Q_0\right)+ F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}
\left(V_j^{(m)};\mathscr Q_m\right).
\end{align*}
Letting $j\to\infty$ in the above inequality and using \eqref{pouxiufu}, we obtain
\begin{align*}
m_{1,\gamma}\left(W_{q_m}\right)&\leq\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,
\gamma}\left(V_j^{(m)};Q_0\right)
\leq m_{1,\gamma}\left(W_{\widetilde q}\right)+\varepsilon+\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}
F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}\left(V_j^{(m)};\mathscr Q_m\right).
\end{align*}
Thus, to prove \eqref{poushen}, we just need to show that
\begin{align}\label{yous}
\lim_{j\to\infty} F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}\left(V_j^{(m)};
\mathscr Q_m\right)=0.
\end{align}
For any distinct $\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal A_m$, define
\begin{align*}
E_{\alpha,\beta,j}^{(m)}
:=\left\{(x,y)\in Q_\alpha\times Q_\beta:
|x-y|<\frac{\rho}{m},\
\frac{\left|V_j^{(m)}(x)-V_j^{(m)}(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\gamma+1}}
>\Lambda_j^{(m)}\right\}.
\end{align*}
Then, we have
\begin{align}\label{zhoupo}
F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}
\left(V_j^{(m)};\mathscr Q_m\right)&\leq
\Lambda_j^{(m)}
\sum_{\substack{\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal A_m\\ \alpha\neq\beta}}
\iint_{E_{\alpha,\beta,j}^{(m)}}|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx
+\Lambda_j^{(m)}\underset{|x-y|\geq\frac{\rho}{m}}{\int_{Q_0}\int_{Q_0}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx\notag\\
&\lesssim\Lambda_j^{(m)}
\sum_{\substack{\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal A_m\\\alpha\neq\beta}}
\iint_{E_{\alpha,\beta,j}^{(m)}}|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx+\Lambda_j^{(m)}\left(\frac{\rho}{m}\right)^\gamma.
\end{align}
Let $(x,y)\in E_{\alpha,\beta,j}^{(m)}$.
For any $\alpha\in\mathcal A_m$, define
\begin{align*}
Q_{\alpha,\rho}:=\left\{x\in Q_\alpha:\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial Q_\alpha)>\frac{\rho}{m}\right\}.
\end{align*}
Since $\alpha\not=\beta,$ it follow that
\begin{align*}
\max\left\{\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial Q_\alpha),\operatorname{dist}(y,\partial Q_\alpha)\right\}
&\leq |x-y|<\frac{\rho}{m}.
\end{align*}
Thus, $(x,y)\in(Q_\alpha\setminus Q_{\alpha,\rho})
\times(Q_\beta\setminus Q_{\beta,\rho}).$
On the other hand, by  \eqref{jinbian}, we find  that $V_j^{(m)}=W_{q_m}$ on
$Q_\alpha\setminus Q_{\alpha,\rho}$. Consequently,
\begin{align*}
\frac{\left|W_{q_m}(x)-W_{q_m}(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\gamma+1}}
=\frac{\left|V_j^{(m)}(x)-V_j^{(m)}(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\gamma+1}}>\Lambda_j^{(m)}
\end{align*}
and hence
\begin{align*}
E_{\alpha,\beta,j}^{(m)}\subset\left\{(x,y)\in Q_\alpha\times Q_\beta:
\frac{\left|W_{q_m}(x)-W_{q_m}(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\gamma+1}}>\Lambda_j^{(m)}\right\}.
\end{align*}
This further implies that
\begin{align*}
\Lambda_j^{(m)}\sum_{\substack{\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal A_m\\\alpha\neq\beta}}
\iint_{E_{\alpha,\beta,j}^{(m)}}|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx
\leq F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}\left(W_{q_m};\mathscr Q_m\right),
\end{align*}
which, combined with \eqref{zhoupo}, implies that
\begin{align*}
F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}\left(V_j^{(m)};\mathscr Q_m\right)
\lesssim
F_{\Lambda_j^{(m)},1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}\left(W_{q_m};\mathscr Q_m\right)
+\Lambda_j^{(m)}\left(\frac{\rho}{m}\right)^\gamma.
\end{align*}
As is showed in the proof of \cite[Proposition 4.11]{lyyyz26},
all  the assumptions of Lemma~\ref{lem-skeleton} are satisfied. Using this and
applying Lemma~\ref{lem-skeleton}  with $U:=W_{q_{m}}$
and $\mathscr Q:=\mathscr Q_m$, we find that
\begin{align*}
\lim_{\lambda\to0^+}F_{\lambda,1,\gamma}^{\rm cross}\left(W_{q_m};\mathscr Q_m\right)=0.
\end{align*}
This shows \eqref{yous}, which completes the proof of Proposition \ref{prop-profend}.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem-tangent}
Let $\Omega\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded open set, and
$u\in BV(\Omega)$. Then, for $|Du|$-a.e. $x\in\Omega$, there exist
an orthogonal matrix $R_x\in\mathcal O(N)$, a sequence
$\{r_k\}_{k\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ with $r_k\to 0$, and a
nondecreasing function $q_x\in BV(I_0)$ such that the following
statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[{\rm(i)}]
\item For any $k\in\mathbb{N},$ it holds that
$\overline{Q_{r_k}^{R_x}(x)}\subset\Omega$,
$|Du|(Q^{R_x}_{r_k}(x))\in (0,\infty)$, and
$|Du|(\partial Q_{r_k}^{R_x}(x))=0.$

\item  $|DW_{q_x}|(Q_0)=|Dq_x|(I_0)=1$.

\item $\lim_{k\to\infty}u_{x,r_k}^{R_x}=W_{q_x}$ in $L^1(Q_0)$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Let
\begin{align*}
L:=\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)\in[0,\infty].
\end{align*}
If $L=\infty$, then the desired conclusion holds automatically. We now assume $L<\infty$.
By Proposition \ref{prop-coerc}, we conclude that $u\in BV(\Omega)$.
Let $\mu:=|Du|$. If $\mu(\Omega)=0$, then \eqref{duanxia} follows from
the nonnegativity of $F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}$.
Hence we assume that $\mu(\Omega)>0$.
Let $A_1\subset\Omega$ be a Borel set such that $\mu(\Omega\setminus A_1)=0$
and Lemma \ref{lem-tangent} holds at every point of $A_1$.
Fix $\varepsilon\in(0,C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}/2)$.
For each $x\in A_1$, let $R_x\in\mathcal O(N)$ and
$\{r_k\}_{k\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$, with $r_k\to0$ as $k\to\infty$, be as in
Lemma \ref{lem-tangent}. Arguing as in the proof of \cite[ (4.60), (4.61), and (4.62)]{lyyyz26},
and using Lemmas \ref{lem-scaling} and \ref{lem-mplsc} together with
Proposition \ref{prop-profend}, we find that there exists
$k_\varepsilon(x)\in\mathbb N$ such that, for any $k\geq k_\varepsilon(x)$,
\begin{align}\label{eq-bvlocal}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(u_j;Q_{r_k}^{R_x}(x)\right)
\geq\left(C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}-\varepsilon\right)\mu\left(Q_{r_k}^{R_x}(x)\right),
\end{align}
where $Q_k:=Q_{r_k}^{R_x}(x).$ Let
\begin{align*}
V_\varepsilon:=\left\{Q_{r_k}^{R_x}(x):x\in A_1,\ k\geq k_\varepsilon(x)\right\},
\end{align*}
where the sequence $\{r_k\}_{k\in\mathbb N}$ is chosen at each $x$ as above.
Using \eqref{eq-bvlocal} and a Morse type covering argument similar to that
used in the proof of \cite[Theorem~4.2]{lyyyz26}, we find that
\eqref{duanxia} holds.
This completes the proof of Theorem \ref{thm-onelinf}.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{thm-limsup}
Let $N\in\mathbb N$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\gamma\in(-\infty,0)$, and
$\Omega\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded open interval when $N=1$ or a
bounded Lipschitz domain when $N\geq2$.
\begin{itemize}
\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] Let $u\in W^{1,p}(\Omega)$ and
$\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy
$\lim_{j\to\infty}\lambda_j=0$. Then there exists
$\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(\Omega)$ satisfying $u_j\to u$ in
$L^p(\Omega)$ and
\begin{align*}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)
\leq C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}\int_\Omega|\nabla u|^p\,dx,
\end{align*}
where $C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}$ is as in \eqref{baochang}.

\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] Let $u\in BV(\Omega)$ and
$\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy
$\lim_{j\to\infty}\lambda_j=0$. Then there exists
$\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^1(\Omega)$ satisfying $u_j\to u$ in
$L^1(\Omega)$ and
\begin{align*}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)
\leq C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\rm cell}|Du|(\Omega).
\end{align*}
\end{itemize}
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{def-pa}
Let $N\in\mathbb N$, $a\in\mathbb R^N\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}$ and $c\in\mathbb R$.
A set of the form $\{x\in\mathbb R^N:a\cdot x\leq c\}$ is called a
\emph{closed affine half-space} in $\mathbb R^N$. A \emph{closed convex
polyhedron} $P$ in $\mathbb R^N$ is an intersection of finitely many closed
affine half-spaces with nonempty interior.
Let $U\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded convex open set. A continuous function
$w:U\to\mathbb R$ is said to be \emph{continuous finite piecewise affine} if
there are finitely many closed convex polyhedra $\{P_i\}_{i=1}^s$ with
pairwise disjoint interiors, vectors $\{\xi_i\}_{i=1}^s$, and numbers
$\{b_i\}_{i=1}^s$ such that $U\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^sP_i$ and, for any
$i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$ and any $x\in U\cap P_i$,
\begin{align*}
w(x)=\xi_i\cdot x+b_i.
\end{align*}
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
Let $U\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded convex open set and
$w:U\to\mathbb R$ be continuous finite piecewise affine. By the assumption
that $U$ is convex and Definition \ref{def-pa}, we conclude that, for any $x,y\in U$,
\begin{align}\label{kong}
|w(x)-w(y)|\leq L|x-y|,
\end{align}
where $L:=\max_{1\leq i\leq s}|\xi_i|$.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- proposition-4: body_md -->
\label{prop-pa}
Let $N\in\mathbb N$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\gamma\in(-\infty,0)$, and
$\Omega\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded open set.
Assume that $U\supset\overline\Omega$ is a bounded convex open set and
$w:U\to\mathbb R$ is continuous finite piecewise affine. Let
$\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy $\lambda_j\to0$ as
$j\to\infty$. Then there exists $\{w_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(\Omega)$ satisfying
$w_j\to w$ in $L^p(\Omega)$ as $j\to\infty$ and
\begin{align}\label{fenshang}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(w_j;\Omega\right)
\leq C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}\int_\Omega|\nabla w|^p\,dx.
\end{align}
<!-- end proposition-4 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{prop-repair}
Let $p\in[1,\infty)$ and $\gamma\in(-\infty,0)$.
For any $j\in\mathbb{N},$ let $\rho_j:=\frac{1}{100j}.$
Then there exist sequences $\{\Lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ and
$\{V_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(Q_0)$ having the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}[{\rm(i)}]
\item $\lim_{j\to\infty}\Lambda_j=0$, $\lim_{j\to\infty}V_j=\ell$ in $L^p(Q_0)$, and
\begin{align}\label{xiuzhu}
\lim_{j\to\infty}F_{\Lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(V_j;Q_0\right)=C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}.
\end{align}
\item For any $j\in\mathbb N$,
\begin{align}\label{xiubian}
V_j=\ell\quad\text{on}\quad
\left\{z\in Q_0:\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)<\rho_j\right\}.
\end{align}
\item It holds that $\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac{\Lambda_j^{-\frac{p}{\gamma}}}{\rho_j}=0.$
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Let $\{\widetilde\Lambda_n\}_{n\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ and $\{v_n\}_{n\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(Q_0)$
be as in Lemma \ref{lem-cellbds}(ii). Define the $1$-Lipschitz function $\theta:\mathbb R\to[0,1]$ by
setting, for any $t\in\mathbb R$,
\begin{align*}
\theta(t):=
\begin{cases}
0,&t\in(-\infty,3],\\
t-3,&t\in(3,4),\\
1,&t\in[4,\infty),
\end{cases}
\end{align*}
and, for any $z\in Q_0$, let
\begin{align*}
\eta_j(z):=\theta\left(\frac{\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)}{\rho_j}\right)=
\begin{cases}
0,&\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)\leq3\rho_j,\\
\displaystyle\frac{\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)}{\rho_j}-3,
&3\rho_j<\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)<4\rho_j,\\
1,&\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)\geq4\rho_j.
\end{cases}
\end{align*}
Then, for any $z,w\in Q_0$,
\begin{align*}
|\eta_j(z)-\eta_j(w)|
&=\left|\theta\left(\frac{\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)}{\rho_j}\right)
-\theta\left(\frac{\operatorname{dist}(w,\partial Q_0)}{\rho_j}\right)\right|\\
&\leq\left|\frac{\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)}{\rho_j}
-\frac{\operatorname{dist}(w,\partial Q_0)}{\rho_j}\right|
\leq\frac{|z-w|}{\rho_j}.
\end{align*}
Thus, $\eta_j$ is a Lipschitz function on $Q_0$ with Lipschitz constant at most $\frac1{\rho_j}$.
For any $j\in\mathbb N$, let
\begin{align*}
E_j:=\left\{z\in Q_0:\eta_j(z)<1\right\}.
\end{align*}
Then $E_j$ is contained in $\{z\in Q_0:\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)<4\rho_j\}$.
Thus, $|E_j|\leq C_N\rho_j$.

Now, for any $j,n\in\mathbb N$, let $V_{j,n}:=\eta_jv_n+(1-\eta_j)\ell$
and $\Lambda_{j,n}:=\frac{\widetilde\Lambda_n}{\alpha_j}$,
where $\{\alpha_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,1)$ satisfies $\lim_{j\to\infty}\alpha_j=1$.
Applying (i) and (ii) of Lemma \ref{lem-cutoff}, we find that, for any $j,n\in\mathbb N$,
\begin{align}\label{xiuneng}
F_{\Lambda_{j,n},p,\gamma}\left(V_{j,n};Q_0\right)
&\leq\frac{F_{\alpha_j\Lambda_{j,n},p,\gamma}(v_n;Q_0)}{\alpha_j^p}
+\frac{F_{\alpha_j\Lambda_{j,n},p,\gamma}(\ell;E_j,Q_0)}{\alpha_j^p}
+\frac{\mathcal H^{N-1}\left(\mathbb S^{N-1}\right)\left\|v_n-\ell\right\|_{L^p(Q_0)}^p}
{|\gamma|(1-\alpha_j)^p\rho_j^p}\notag\\
&\leq\frac{F_{\widetilde\Lambda_n,p,\gamma}(v_n;Q_0)}{\alpha_j^p}
+\frac{C_{N,\gamma}\rho_j}{\alpha_j^p}
+\frac{\mathcal H^{N-1}\left(\mathbb S^{N-1}\right)\left\|v_n-\ell\right\|_{L^p(Q_0)}^p}
{|\gamma|(1-\alpha_j)^p\rho_j^p}.
\end{align}
From Lemma \ref{lem-cellbds}(ii), we infer that
$\lim_{n\to\infty}v_n=\ell$ in $L^p(Q_0)$ and $\lim_{n\to\infty}\widetilde{\Lambda_n}=0.$ Thus,
 for any given $j\in\mathbb N$, $\lim_{n\to\infty}V_{j,n}=\ell$ in $L^p(Q_0)$,
$\lim_{n\to\infty}\Lambda_{j,n}=0$, and
$\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\Lambda_{j,n}^{-\frac{p}{\gamma}}}{\rho_j}=0.$
This, combined with \eqref{xiuneng} and the
definition of $C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}},$
 implies that, for any given $j\in\mathbb N$, there exists
$n(j)\in\mathbb N$ such that
\begin{align*}
\left\|V_{j,n(j)}-\ell\right\|_{L^p(Q_0)}\leq\frac1j,\ \
\Lambda_{j,n(j)}\leq\frac1j,\ \
\frac{\Lambda_{j,n(j)}^{-\frac{p}{\gamma}}}{\rho_j}\leq\frac1j,
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
F_{\Lambda_{j,n(j)},p,\gamma}\left(V_{j,n(j)};Q_0\right)
\leq\frac{C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}+\frac1j}{\alpha_j^p}
+\frac{C_{N,\gamma}\rho_j}{\alpha_j^p}+\frac1j.
\end{align*}
Finally, for any $j\in\mathbb N$, let
$\Lambda_j:=\Lambda_{j,n(j)}$ and $V_j:=V_{j,n(j)}$.
Letting $j\to\infty$, we then conclude that
\begin{align*}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\Lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(V_j;Q_0\right)
\leq C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}.
\end{align*}
On the other hand, since $C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}=m_{p,\gamma}(\ell)$, it follows that
\begin{align*}
C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}
\leq\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\Lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(V_j;Q_0\right)
\end{align*}
and hence \eqref{xiuzhu} holds. The other assertions of the present
proposition follow from the construction and the choice of $n(j)$. This completes the proof
of Proposition \ref{prop-repair}.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Let $\{P_i\}_{i=1}^s$, $\{\xi_i\}_{i=1}^s$, and
$\{b_i\}_{i=1}^s$ be as in Definition \ref{def-pa}.
For any $i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$, let
$D_i:=\Omega\cap \operatorname{int}(P_i).$
Since the interiors of the polyhedra are pairwise disjoint and
$U\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^sP_i$, it follows that
\begin{align}\label{papart}
\left|\Omega\setminus\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^sD_i\right)\right|=0.
\end{align}
We show the present proposition under the additional assumption
\begin{align}\label{feiling}
 a_*:=\min_{1\leq i\leq s}|\xi_i|>0.
\end{align}
This assumption can be removed by the same perturbation and diagonal argument as in the
proof of \cite[Proposition~3.7]{lyyyz26}; we omit the detail here.
Let $\{\Lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset (0,\infty),$
$\{V_j\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset L^p(Q_0)$, and $\{\rho_j\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset (0,\infty)$
be as in Proposition \ref{prop-repair}.
By the fact that $\lim_{j\to\infty}\lambda_j=0$,  we find that  there exists  a sequence
$\{k_j\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset \mathbb{N}$ such that
\begin{align}\label{paidx}
\lim_{j\to\infty}k_j=\infty\quad\text{and}\quad
\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_j}{\Lambda_{k_j}}=0.
\end{align}
For any $j\in\mathbb N$, let $\widehat\Lambda_j:=\Lambda_{k_j}$,
$\widehat V_j:=V_{k_j}$, and $\widehat\rho_j:=\rho_{k_j}$.
Since $\lim_{j\to\infty}\widehat{\rho}_j=0,$
after discarding finitely many indices if necessary,
we may assume that, for any $j\in\mathbb{N},$
$0<\widehat\rho_j<\frac{1}{2}$. Let  $i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$ and $j\in\mathbb{N}.$ Define
\begin{align}\label{pah}
h_{j,i}:=\left(\frac{|\xi_i|\widehat\Lambda_j}{\lambda_j}\right)^{\frac{p}{\gamma}}.
\end{align}
From \eqref{paidx}, we deduce that
\begin{align}\label{pahzero}
\lim_{j\to\infty}\max_{1\leq i\leq s}h_{j,i}=0.
\end{align}
Choose  matrix $R_i\in\mathcal O(N)$ such that $R_ie_N=\frac{\xi_i}{|\xi_i|},$ where
$i\in\{1,\ldots, s\}$ and $e_N:=(0,\ldots,0,1)$. For any $j\in\mathbb{N}$,
$i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$, and $k\in\mathbb Z^N$, define
\begin{align}\label{shui}
Q_{j,i,k}:=h_{j,i}R_i(k+Q_0)=h_{j,i}R_ik+h_{j,i}R_iQ_0.
\end{align}
Let $\mathscr Q_{j,i}$ denote the finite family of those cubes whose
closures are contained in $D_i$. Let $Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}$. By \eqref{shui}, we conclude that
$Q=x_Q+h_{j,i}R_iQ_0$, where $x_Q$ is the center of $Q$.
Thus, for any $x\in Q$, there exists a unique $z\in Q_0$ such that
$x=x_Q+h_{j,i}R_iz$. In what follows, for $Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}$ and $x\in Q$, the
corresponding point $z\in Q_0$ is always understood to be given by
$z=R_i^{-1}\frac{x-x_Q}{h_{j,i}}$ or, equivalently, $x=x_Q+h_{j,i}R_iz$.
Recall that, for any $z\in\mathbb{R}^N,$ $\ell(z):=z_N.$
From Definition \ref{def-pa} and the definition of $R_i$, we infer that, for any $x\in Q$,
\begin{align*}
w(x)&=w(x_Q)+\xi_i\cdot(x-x_Q)=w(x_Q)+h_{j,i}\xi_i\cdot R_i z
=w(x_Q)+h_{j,i}(R_i^{-1}\xi_i)\cdot z\notag\\
&=w(x_Q)+|\xi_i|h_{j,i}e_N\cdot z=w(x_Q)+|\xi_i|h_{j,i}\ell(z).
\end{align*}
Now define $w_j$ as follows.  For any $i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$, $Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}$,
and $x\in Q$, let
\begin{align}\label{padef}
w_j(x)&:=w(x_Q)+|\xi_i|h_{j,i}\widehat V_j
\left(R_i^{-1}\frac{x-x_Q}{h_{j,i}}\right)=w(x_Q)+|\xi_i|h_{j,i}\widehat V_j(z),
\end{align}
and, for any $x\in\Omega\setminus\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^s\bigcup_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}Q\right),$
let $w_j(x)=w(x)$. Let $n_{j,i}:=\#\mathscr Q_{j,i},$ which denotes the number of cubes in the finite
family $\mathscr Q_{j,i}$. Since $\mathscr Q_{j,i}$ consists of cubes of edge length $h_{j,i}$
whose closures are contained in $D_i$, it follows that, for any $i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$,
\begin{align*}
D_i\setminus\left(\bigcup_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}\overline{Q}\right)\subset\left\{
x\in D_i:\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathbb R^N\setminus D_i)\leq\sqrt N\,h_{j,i}\right\}.
\end{align*}
This, together with \eqref{pahzero}, implies that
\begin{align}\label{pafill}
\lim_{j\to\infty}\left|D_i\setminus\left(\bigcup_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}Q\right)\right|=0
\end{align}
and hence
\begin{align}\label{pavol}
\lim_{j\to\infty}n_{j,i}h_{j,i}^N=|D_i|.
\end{align}
By this,  the change-of-variables formula, \eqref{padef}, \eqref{pahzero},
and the fact that $\lim_{j\to\infty}\widehat{V}_j=\ell$ in $L^p(Q_0),$ we conclude that
\begin{align*}
\|w_j-w\|_{L^p(\Omega)}^p&=\sum_{i=1}^s\sum_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}
|\xi_i|^ph_{j,i}^{N+p}\left\|\widehat V_j-\ell\right\|_{L^p(Q_0)}^p\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^s|\xi_i|^pn_{j,i}h_{j,i}^{N+p}\left\|\widehat V_j-\ell\right\|_{L^p(Q_0)}^p\to0
\end{align*}
as $j\to\infty$. Thus,  to prove the  present proposition under the assumption \eqref{feiling},
it remains to show \eqref{fenshang}.

For any $j\in\mathbb N$, define $A_j:=\bigcup_{i=1}^s\bigcup_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}(Q\times Q)$
and
\begin{align*}
R_j:=\lambda_j^p
\underset{|w_j(x)-w_j(y)|>\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}}
{\iint_{(\Omega\times\Omega)\setminus A_j}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx.
\end{align*}
Since the cubes are pairwise disjoint, it follows that, for any $j\in\mathbb{N},$
\begin{align}\label{haq}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(w_j;Q\right)=\sum_{i=1}^s\sum_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(w_j;Q\right)+R_j.
\end{align}
From Lemma \ref{lem-scaling} and \eqref{padef}, we deduce that,  for any  $i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$,
$j\in\mathbb N$, and $Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}$,
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(w_j;Q\right)=|\xi_i|^ph_{j,i}^N
F_{\widehat\Lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(\widehat V_j;Q_0\right).
\end{align*}
By this, \eqref{pavol}, and Proposition \ref{prop-repair}, we obtain
\begin{align*}
\lim_{j\to\infty}\sum_{i=1}^s\sum_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(w_j;Q\right)
&=\lim_{j\to\infty}F_{\widehat\Lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(\widehat V_j;Q_0\right)
\sum_{i=1}^s|\xi_i|^pn_{j,i}h_{j,i}^N\notag\\
&=C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}\sum_{i=1}^s|\xi_i|^p|D_i|
=C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}\int_\Omega|\nabla w|^p\,dx.
\end{align*}
This, combined with \eqref{haq}, implies that, to prove \eqref{fenshang}, it suffices to show that
$\lim_{j\to\infty}R_j=0.$ From \eqref{pah}, Proposition \ref{prop-repair}(iii), and \eqref{feiling},
we infer that, for any $i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$,
\begin{align*}
\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_j^{-\frac{p}{\gamma}}}{\widehat\rho_jh_{j,i}}
=\lim_{j\to\infty}|\xi_i|^{-\frac{p}{\gamma}}
\frac{\widehat\Lambda_j^{-\frac{p}{\gamma}}}{\widehat\rho_j}=0.
\end{align*}
Thus, we can choose $\{d_j\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset (0,\infty)$ such that,
for any $i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$ and $j\in\mathbb{N},$
\begin{align}\label{rt}
d_j<  \widehat\rho_jh_{j,i}
\end{align}
and
\begin{align}\label{lo}
\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_j^{-\frac{p}{\gamma}}}{d_j}=0
\end{align}
On the other hand, by Proposition \ref{prop-repair}(iii),
we find that $\lim_{j\to\infty}\widehat{\rho_j}=0$. This, together with \eqref{rt}, implies that
$\lim_{j\to\infty}\frac{d_j}{h_{j,i}}=0.$
Let $j\in\mathbb{N}$. Define
\begin{align*}
R_j^{\rm near}:=\lambda_j^p\underset{\substack{|x-y|<d_j\\
|w_j(x)-w_j(y)|>\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}}}
{\iint_{(\Omega\times\Omega)\setminus A_j}}|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
R_j^{\rm far}:=\lambda_j^p\underset{\substack{|x-y|\geq d_j\\
|w_j(x)-w_j(y)|>\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}}}
{\iint_{(\Omega\times\Omega)\setminus A_j}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx.
\end{align*}
Then $R_j=R_j^{\rm near}+R_j^{\rm far}$ and
\begin{align*}
R_j^{\rm far}&\leq\lambda_j^p\underset{|x-y|\geq d_j}
{\int_\Omega\int_\Omega}|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx
\leq\lambda_j^p|\Omega|\int_{|h|\geq d_j}|h|^{\gamma-N}\,dh
=\frac{\mathcal H^{N-1}(\mathbb S^{N-1})}{|\gamma|}|\Omega|\lambda_j^pd_j^{\gamma}.
\end{align*}
From this and \eqref{lo}, it follows that
$\lim_{j\to\infty} R_j^{\rm far}=0.$

Next, we estimate $R_j^{\rm near}$.
For any $i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$ and $Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}$, define
\begin{align*}
Q^{\rm in}:=\left\{x\in Q:\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial Q)>d_j\right\}
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
E_j:=\Omega\setminus\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^s
\bigcup_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}Q^{\rm in}\right)=\bigcap_{i=1}^{s}\bigcap_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}
\left(\Omega\setminus Q^{\rm in}\right).
\end{align*}
Then
\begin{align}\label{jindui}
\left\{(x,y)\in(\Omega\times\Omega)\setminus A_j:
|x-y|<d_j\right\}\subset E_j\times E_j.
\end{align}
By \eqref{rt}, we conclude that, for any $x\in Q\setminus Q^{\rm in}$ with $x=x_Q+h_{j,i}R_iz$,
\begin{align*}
\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial Q_0)=\frac{\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial Q)}{h_{j,i}}
\leq\frac{d_j}{h_{j,i}}<\widehat\rho_j.
\end{align*}
This, combined with  \eqref{xiubian} and \eqref{padef}, imply that, for any $x\in Q\setminus Q^{\rm in}$,
\begin{align*}
w_j(x)=w(x_Q)+|\xi_i|h_{j,i}\widehat V_j(z)=w(x_Q)+|\xi_i|h_{j,i}\ell(z)=w(x).
\end{align*}
On the other hand, from the definitions of $w_j$ and $w$, we infer that
$w_j=w$ on $\Omega\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^s\bigcup_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}Q$,
which, together with the fact that
\begin{align}\label{youshang}
E_j=\left[\Omega\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^s
\bigcup_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}Q\right]
\cup\left[\bigcup_{i=1}^s\bigcup_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}
\left(Q\setminus Q^{\rm in}\right)\right],
\end{align}
further implies that $w_j=w$ on $E_j$. Using this, \eqref{jindui},
