and \eqref{kong}, we find that
\begin{align*}
&\left\{(x,y)\in(\Omega\times\Omega)\setminus A_j:|x-y|<d_j,\
|w_j(x)-w_j(y)|>\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}\right\}\\
&\quad\subset\left\{(x,y)\in E_j\times E_j:
\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}<|w(x)-w(y)|\leq L|x-y|\right\}\\
&\quad\subset\left\{(x,y)\in E_j\times E_j:
|x-y|>\left(\frac{\lambda_j}{L}\right)^{-\frac{p}{\gamma}}\right\},
\end{align*}
where we  used $\gamma\in(-\infty,0)$ in the last step. Thus,
\begin{align}\label{sad}
R_j^{\rm near}\leq\lambda_j^p
\underset{|x-y|>\left(\frac{\lambda_j}{L}\right)^{-\frac{p}{\gamma}}}
{\int_{E_j}\int_{E_j}}|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx
\leq\frac{\mathcal H^{N-1}(\mathbb S^{N-1})}{|\gamma|}L^p|E_j|.
\end{align}
Using \eqref{youshang}, \eqref{pafill}, and \eqref{papart}, we obtain
\begin{align*}
|E_j|&=\left|\Omega\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^s\bigcup_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}Q\right|
+\sum_{i=1}^s\sum_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}|Q\setminus Q^{\rm in}|\notag\\
&=\left|\Omega\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^s
\bigcup_{Q\in\mathscr Q_{j,i}}Q\right|
+\sum_{i=1}^s n_{j,i}h_{j,i}^N
\left[1-\left(1-\frac{2d_j}{h_{j,i}}\right)^N\right]\to 0
\end{align*}
as $j\to\infty.$ From this and \eqref{sad}, it follows that
$\lim_{j\to\infty}  R_j^{\rm near}=0.$
This completes the proof of Proposition \ref{prop-pa}.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lem-padensity}
Let $N\in\mathbb N$, $p\in [1,\infty)$, $\Omega\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded open interval
when $N=1$ or a bounded Lipschitz domain when $N\ge2$, and $u\in W^{1,p}(\Omega)$.  Then there exist a bounded
convex open set $U$ containing $\overline\Omega$ and a sequence
$\{u_m\}_{m\in\mathbb N}\subset C(U)$ of continuous finite
piecewise-affine functions such that $\lim_{m\to\infty}u_m= u$ in $W^{1,p}(\Omega).$
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lem-strictapp}
Let $N\in\mathbb N$, $\Omega\subset \mathbb{R}^N$ be an open set, and $u\in BV(\Omega)$.
Then there  exists a sequence $\{u_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset W^{1,1}(\Omega)\cap C^\infty(\Omega)$ such that
$\lim_{m\to\infty}u_m=u$ in $L^1(\Omega)$ and
$$\lim_{m\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u_m(x)|\,dx=|Du|(\Omega).$$
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
We first show (i). Let  $u\in W^{1,p}(\Omega)$ and let
$\{u_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}$ be as in Lemma \ref{lem-padensity}.
By Proposition \ref{prop-pa}, we  conclude that, for any given
$m\in\mathbb{N}$, there exists a sequence $\{u_j^{(m)}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset L^p(\Omega)$ such that
$u_j^{(m)}\to u_m$ in $L^p(\Omega)$ as $j\to\infty$ and
\begin{align*}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty} F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j^{(m)};\Omega\right)\leq
C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}\int_\Omega|\nabla u_m|^p\,dx.
\end{align*}
Thus, for any given $m\in\mathbb{N},$ there  exists  $J_m\in\mathbb{N}$ such that, for any $j\geq J_m$,
$\|u_j^{(m)}-u_m\|_{L^p(\Omega)}\leq\frac1m$ and
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j^{(m)};\Omega\right)\leq C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}
\int_\Omega\left|\nabla u_m\right|^p\,dx+\frac1m.
\end{align*}
Without loss of generality, we may assume that $\{J_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}$ is strictly increasing.
Let   $j\geq J_1$, and let $m(j)$ be the unique integer such that $J_{m(j)}\leq j<J_{m(j)+1}.$
Define $u_j:=u_j^{(m(j))}.$ Since $\{J_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}$ is strictly increasing,
it follows that  $\lim_{j\to\infty}m(j)=\infty.$ Then we have
\begin{align*}
\|u_j-u\|_{L^p(\Omega)}\leq
\left\|u_j^{(m(j))}-u_{m(j)}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega)}
+\left\|u_{m(j)}-u\right\|_{L^p(\Omega)}\leq\frac1{m(j)}+\left\|u_{m(j)}-u\right\|_{L^p(\Omega)}
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)\leq C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}
\int_\Omega\left|\nabla u_{m(j)}\right|^p\,dx+\frac1{m(j)}.
\end{align*}
Letting $j\to\infty$ in the above estimates, we find that $\lim_{j\to\infty}u_j=u$ in $L^p(\Omega)$ and the
desired inequality holds, which proves (i).

We now prove (ii). Let $u\in BV(\Omega)$, and let $\{u_m\}_{m\in\mathbb N}$ be as in
Lemma \ref{lem-strictapp}. Then $u_m\to u$ in $L^1(\Omega)$ as $m\to\infty$ and
\begin{align*}
\lim_{m\to\infty}\int_\Omega|\nabla u_m|\,dx=|Du|(\Omega).
\end{align*}
Applying (i) with $p=1$ to each $u_m$ and the given sequence
$\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}$, we conclude that, for any given
$m\in\mathbb N$, there exists a sequence
$\{u_j^{(m)}\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^1(\Omega)$ such that
$u_j^{(m)}\to u_m$ in $L^1(\Omega)$ and
\begin{align*}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(u_j^{(m)};\Omega\right)
\leq C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}\int_\Omega|\nabla u_m|\,dx.
\end{align*}
Thus, for any given $m\in\mathbb N$, there exists $J_m\in\mathbb N$
such that, for any $j\geq J_m$,
$\|u_j^{(m)}-u_m\|_{L^1(\Omega)}\leq\frac1m$ and
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(u_j^{(m)};\Omega\right)
\leq C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}\int_\Omega|\nabla u_m|\,dx+\frac1m.
\end{align*}
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
$\{J_m\}_{m\in\mathbb N}$ is strictly increasing.
For $j\geq J_1$, let $m(j)$ be the unique integer such that
$J_{m(j)}\leq j<J_{m(j)+1}$ and define $u_j:=u_j^{(m(j))}$.
Since $\{J_m\}_{m\in\mathbb N}$ is strictly increasing, it follows that
$\lim_{j\to\infty}m(j)=\infty$. Then, for any $j\geq J_1$,
\begin{align*}
\|u_j-u\|_{L^1(\Omega)}&\leq\left\|u_j^{(m(j))}-u_{m(j)}\right\|_{L^1(\Omega)}
+\left\|u_{m(j)}-u\right\|_{L^1(\Omega)}\\
&\leq\frac1{m(j)}+\left\|u_{m(j)}-u\right\|_{L^1(\Omega)}
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)\leq C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}
\int_\Omega|\nabla u_{m(j)}|\,dx+\frac1{m(j)}.
\end{align*}
Letting $j\to\infty$ in the above estimates and using Lemma \ref{lem-strictapp},
we conclude that $u_j\to u$ in $L^1(\Omega)$ as $j\to\infty$ and
\begin{align*}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,\gamma}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)
\leq C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\mathrm{cell}}|Du|(\Omega).
\end{align*}
This proves (ii) and completes the proof of Theorem \ref{thm-limsup}.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{lem-critsmth}
Let $N\in\mathbb{N}$, let $p\in[1,\infty)$, and let $U\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded open interval when $N=1$ or a bounded smooth domain when $N\geq2$.
Then there exists a positive constant $\kappa_{N,p}$, independent of $U$,
such that the family $F_{\lambda,p,-p}(\cdot;U)$ converges, in the sense of
$\Gamma$-convergence in $L^p(U)$, as $\lambda\to0^+$, to the functional defined by setting, for any $u\in L^p(U)$,
\begin{align*}
u\longmapsto
\begin{cases}
\kappa_{N,p}\displaystyle\int_U|\nabla u|^p\,dx,
&p\in(1,\infty)\ \hbox{and}\ u\in W^{1,p}(U),\\[2mm]
\kappa_{N,1}|Du|(U),
&p=1\ \hbox{and}\ u\in BV(U),\\[1mm]
\infty,&\hbox{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{prop-critdom}
Let $N\in\mathbb{N}$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, and $\Omega\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a
bounded open interval when $N=1$ or a bounded Lipschitz domain when $N\geq2$.
Then the family $F_{\lambda,p,-p}(\cdot;\Omega)$ converges, in the sense of
$\Gamma$-convergence in $L^p(\Omega)$, to $\Psi_{p,-p}^{\Omega}$ as
$\lambda\to0^+$.
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
We first identify the cell constant. Choose an increasing sequence
$\{U_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}$ of bounded smooth domains in $Q_0$ such that $\bigcup_{m\in\mathbb N}U_m=Q_0$ (see, for instance, \cite[Proposition~8.2.1]{d08}). Let $\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy $\lambda_j\to0$ as $j\to\infty$. Let
$\{v_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(Q_0)$ satisfy
$v_j\to\ell$ in $L^p(Q_0)$ as $j\in\to\infty$. By the
$\Gamma$-liminf inequality in Lemma \ref{lem-critsmth}, we
obtain, for any $m\in\mathbb{N}$,
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,-p}\left(v_j;Q_0\right)
&\geq\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,-p}\left(v_j;U_m\right)
\geq\kappa_{N,p}|U_m|.
\end{align*}
Letting $m\to\infty$ in the above inequality and then taking the infimum over
all admissible sequences in \eqref{baochang}, we find that
$C_{N,p,-p}^{\rm cell}\geq\kappa_{N,p}.$

To prove the reverse inequality, choose a sequence
$\{V_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}$ of bounded smooth domains such
that $Q_0\subset V_m$ for any $m\in\mathbb{N}$ and $|V_m\setminus Q_0|\to0$
as $m\to\infty$ (see, for instance, \cite[Theorem~8.3.1]{d08}).
For any $m\in\mathbb N$, choose a sequence
$\{\lambda_j^{(m)}\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfying
$\lambda_j^{(m)}\to0$ as $j\to\infty$. From the $\Gamma$-limsup inequality in Lemma \ref{lem-critsmth}, it follows that there exists a sequence
$\{w_j^{(m)}\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(V_m)$ satisfying
$w_j^{(m)}\to\ell$ in $L^p(V_m)$ as $j\to\infty$ and
\begin{align*}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j^{(m)},p,-p}\left(w_j^{(m)};V_m\right)
\leq\kappa_{N,p}|V_m|.
\end{align*}
Hence, by \eqref{baochang}, we conclude that
\begin{align}\label{reverse}
C_{N,p,-p}^{\rm cell}\leq\varliminf_{j\to\infty}
F_{\lambda_j^{(m)},p,-p}\left(w_j^{(m)};Q_0\right)
\leq\varlimsup_{j\to\infty} F_{\lambda_j^{(m)},p,-p}\left(w_j^{(m)};V_m\right)
\leq\kappa_{N,p}|V_m|.
\end{align}
Letting $m\to\infty$ in \eqref{reverse} yields the reverse inequality $C_{N,p,-p}^{\rm cell}\leq\kappa_{N,p}.$
Therefore,
\begin{align}\label{yanggu}
C_{N,p,-p}^{\rm cell}=\kappa_{N,p}.
\end{align}

Next, we prove the $\Gamma$-liminf inequality on $\Omega$.
Let $\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy
$\lambda_j\to0$ as $j\to\infty$. Let $u\in L^p(\Omega)$, and let
$\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(\Omega)$ satisfy
$u_j\to u$ in $L^p(\Omega)$ as $j\to\infty$.
Define
\begin{align*}
L:=\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,-p}\left(u_j;\Omega\right).
\end{align*}
There is nothing to prove when $L=\infty$, so we assume that $L<\infty$.
Choose an increasing sequence $\{\Omega_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}$ of bounded smooth domains in $\Omega$ such that $\bigcup_{m\in\mathbb N}\Omega_m=\Omega$
(see, for instance, \cite[Proposition~8.2.1]{d08}).
By Lemma \ref{lem-critsmth}, we find that, for any $m\in\mathbb{N},$
\begin{align}\label{reverse1}
L\geq
\begin{cases}
\kappa_{N,p}\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_m}|\nabla u|^p\,dx,&p\in(1,\infty),\\[2mm]
\kappa_{N,1}|Du|(\Omega_m),&p=1.
\end{cases}
\end{align}
Letting $m\to\infty$ in \eqref{reverse1} and using \eqref{yanggu},
we conclude that
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,-p}\left(u_j;\Omega\right)\geq\Psi_{p,-p}^{\Omega}(u),
\end{align*}
which shows the $\Gamma$-liminf inequality. On the  other hand, from
Theorem \ref{thm-limsup}, we infer that the $\Gamma$-limsup inequality
holds. This completes the proof of Proposition \ref{prop-critdom}.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{lem-zeroreg}
Let $N\in\mathbb{N}$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\gamma\in(-1,0)$, and $U\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a measurable set. Then the family
$F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\cdot;U)$ converges, in the sense of
$\Gamma$-convergence in $L^p(U)$, to the zero functional as $\lambda\to0^+$.
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{lem-posdom}
Let $N\in\mathbb{N}$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\gamma\in(0,\infty)$, and
$\Omega\subset\mathbb R^N$ be a bounded open interval when $N=1$ or a
bounded Lipschitz domain when $N\geq2$. Then the family
$F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\cdot;\Omega)$ converges, in the sense of
$\Gamma$-convergence in $L^p(\Omega)$, to $\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\Omega}$ as
$\lambda\to\infty$.
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
Theorem \ref{thm-domfull} follows from Theorem \ref{thm-negdom}, Proposition \ref{prop-critdom}, and  Lemmas \ref{lem-zeroreg} and \ref{lem-posdom} directly.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
\label{prop-whlzero}
Let $N\in\mathbb{N}$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, and $\gamma\in(-1,0)$. Then the family
$F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\cdot;\mathbb R^N)$ converges, in the sense of
$\Gamma$-convergence in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$, to the zero
functional as $\lambda\to0^+$.
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Since the $\Gamma$-liminf inequality holds automatically,
we just need to prove the $\Gamma$-limsup inequality.
Let $\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy
$\lambda_j\to0$ as $j\to\infty$. Let $u\in L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$.
For any $M\in (0,\infty)$, let $Q_M:=(-M,M)^N$ and
$T_M$ be the truncation operator defined in \eqref{jieding}.
Define $w_M:=\mathbf1_{Q_M}T_M(u).$
Then, for any given $R\in (0,\infty)$ and any $M\in[R,\infty)$,
\begin{align*}
\|w_M-u\|_{L^1(Q_R)}=\|T_M(u)-u\|_{L^1(Q_R)}
\leq\int_{Q_R\cap\left\{|u|>M\right\}}|u|\,dx\to0
\end{align*}
as $M\to\infty$. Thus, $w_M\to u$ in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$
as $M\to\infty$. Using Lemma \ref{lem-zeroreg} with $U:=\mathbb{R}^N$, we
conclude that, for  any given $M\in (0,\infty)$,
there  exists  a sequence
$\{v_j^{(M)}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset L^p(\mathbb{R}^N)$ such that
\begin{align*}
v_j^{(M)}\to w_M\quad\text{in }L^p(\mathbb R^N)
\ \ \text{and}\ \
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(v_j^{(M)};\mathbb R^N\right)\to0
\end{align*}
as $j\to \infty.$
Choosing a strictly increasing sequence $\{J_M\}_{M\in\mathbb{N}}$ of
integers such that, for any given $M\in\mathbb{N}$ and any $j\in\mathbb{N}$ with
$j\geq J_M$,
\begin{align}\label{yuanzi}
\left\|v_j^{(M)}-w_M\right\|_{L^p(\mathbb R^N)}\leq\frac1M
\ \ \text{and}\ \
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(v_j^{(M)};\mathbb R^N\right)\leq\frac1M.
\end{align}
For any $j\in\mathbb{N}$ with  $J_M\leq j<J_{M+1}$,
define $u_j:=v_j^{(M)}$. By \eqref{yuanzi} and H\"older's inequality,
we find that
\begin{align*}
\lim_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_{j};\mathbb R^N\right)=0
\end{align*}
and, for any given $R\in (0,\infty)$ and any
$j\in\mathbb{N}$ with  $J_M\leq j<J_{M+1}$ for some $M\in\mathbb{N}\cap [R,\infty)$,
\begin{align*}
\|u_j-u\|_{L^1(Q_R)}
&\leq |Q_R|^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\left\|v_j^{(M)}-w_M\right\|_{L^p(\mathbb R^N)}
+\|w_M-u\|_{L^1(Q_R)}\\
&\leq\frac{|Q_R|^{1-\frac{1}{p}}}M+\|w_M-u\|_{L^1(Q_R)}\to0
\end{align*}
as $j\to\infty$. This further implies that $u_j\to u$ in $L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^N)$,
which completes the proof of Proposition \ref{prop-whlzero}.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{thm-whlndeg}
Let $N\in\mathbb{N}$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, and $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1]\cup(0,\infty)$. Assume that $(N,p,\gamma)\neq(1,1,-1)$. Then
$F_{\lambda,p,\gamma}(\cdot;\mathbb R^N)$ converges, in the sense of
$\Gamma$-convergence in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$, to
$\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}$ as $\lambda\to\infty$ when $\gamma\in(0,\infty)$ and
as $\lambda\to0^+$ when $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1]$.
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- proposition-8: body_md -->
\label{prop-whllower}
Let $N\in\mathbb{N}$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, and
$\gamma\in(-\infty,-1]\cup(0,\infty)$.
Let $\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy
$\lambda_j\to\infty$ when $\gamma\in(0,\infty)$,  $\lambda_j\to0$ when
$\gamma\in(-\infty,-1]$ as $j\to\infty$. Let $u\in L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$, and let $\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$ satisfy
$u_j\to u$ in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$ as $j\to\infty$. Then
\begin{align}\label{zhengxia}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_{j};\mathbb R^N\right)\geq
\begin{cases}
C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb R^N}|\nabla u|^p\,dx,
&p\in(1,\infty),\ u\in\dot W^{1,p}(\mathbb R^N),\\[2mm]
C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\rm cell}|Du|(\mathbb R^N),
&p=1,\ u\in\dot{BV}(\mathbb R^N),\\[1mm]
\infty,&\text{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\end{align}
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Let
\begin{align*}
L:=\varliminf_{j\to\infty}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_{j};\mathbb R^N\right).
\end{align*}
If $L=\infty$, there is nothing to prove. We now assume $L<\infty$. Passing to a subsequence and relabelling if necessary, we may assume that
\begin{align*}
\lim_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_{j},p,\gamma}\left(u_{j};\mathbb R^N\right)= L.
\end{align*}
Fix $R\in (0,\infty)$ and  $Q_R:=(-R,R)^N$.

We first consider the case $p=1.$ In this case,  by the fact that
$\lim_{j\to\infty}u_j=u$ in $L^1(Q_R)$ and Theorem \ref{thm-domfull}, we  find that $u\in BV(Q_R)$ and $C_{N,1,\gamma}^{\rm cell}|Du|(Q_R)\leq L.$
Letting $R\to\infty$, we prove \eqref{zhengxia} in this case.

Now, let $p\in (1,\infty)$.
Let $M\in (0,\infty)$ and $T_M$ be  as  in
\eqref{jieding}.
Since $u_j\to u$ in $L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^N)$ as $j\to\infty$, it follows that
\begin{align*}
\left\|T_M(u_j)-T_M(u)\right\|_{L^p(Q_R)}^p
\leq(2M)^{p-1}\left\|T_M(u_j)-T_M(u)\right\|_{L^1(Q_R)}\to0
\end{align*}
as $j\to\infty$. By \eqref{deng},  we conclude that, for any $j\in\mathbb{N},$
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(T_M(u_j);Q_R)\leq F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(u_j;Q_R)
\leq F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(u_j;\mathbb R^N).
\end{align*}
This, together with Theorem \ref{thm-domfull}, implies that
$T_M(u)\in W^{1,p}(Q_R)$ and
\begin{align}\label{jiexia}
C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}\int_{Q_R}|\nabla T_M(u)|^p\,dx\leq L.
\end{align}
From this and Poincar\'e's inequality, we infer that
\begin{align*}
\|T_M(u)\|_{L^p(Q_R)}&\leq
\left\|T_M(u)-\frac1{|Q_R|}\int_{Q_R}T_M(u)\,dx\right\|_{L^p(Q_R)}
 +|Q_R|^{\frac{1}{p}}\left|\frac1{|Q_R|}\int_{Q_R}T_M(u)\,dx\right|\notag\\
&\lesssim R\|\nabla T_M(u)\|_{L^p(Q_R)}
 +|Q_R|^{\frac{1}{p}-1}\|u\|_{L^1(Q_R)}\lesssim R\left(\frac{L}{C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}
 +|Q_R|^{\frac{1}{p}-1}\|u\|_{L^1(Q_R)}.
\end{align*}
Thus,
\begin{align*}
\sup_{M\in(0,\infty)}\left\|T_M(u)\right\|_{W^{1,p}(Q_R)}<\infty.
\end{align*}
Using this and the fact that
the space $W^{1,p}(Q_R)$ is reflexive, we conclude that  there
exist a sequence $\{M_k\}_{k\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ and a function
$v\in W^{1,p}(Q_R)$ such that
$M_k\to\infty$ and $T_{M_k}(u)\to v$ weakly in $W^{1,p}(Q_R)$ as $k\to\infty$.
On the other hand,
\begin{align*}
\left\|T_{M_k}(u)-u\right\|_{L^1(Q_R)}\leq\int_{Q_R\cap\left\{|u|>M_k\right\}}|u|\,dx
\to0
\end{align*}
as $k\to\infty$. Thus, $u=v$ and $T_{M_k}(u)\to u$ weakly in $W^{1,p}(Q_R).$
By the weak lower semicontinuity of $L^p$-norm and \eqref{jiexia},
we conclude that
\begin{align}\label{5.3x}
C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}\int_{Q_R}|\nabla u|^p\,dx
&\leq C_{N,p,\gamma}^{\rm cell}\varliminf_{k\to\infty}
\int_{Q_R}|\nabla T_{M_k}(u)|^p\,dx\leq L.
\end{align}
Letting $R\to\infty$ in \eqref{5.3x}, we prove \eqref{zhengxia} in this case.
This completes the proof of Proposition \ref{prop-whllower}.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{lem-oneconst}
Let $N\in\mathbb{N}$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, and $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1]\cup(0,\infty)$. Fix $R\in (0,\infty)$ and $a\in(0,R)$.  Define
\begin{align*}
Q:=(-R,R)^N\ \ \text{and}\ \
\mathcal C_a(Q):=\left\{x\in Q:\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial Q)<a\right\}.
\end{align*}
Suppose that $w\in L^\infty(\mathbb R^N)$ satisfies $w|_Q\in W^{1,p}(Q)$
if $p\in(1,\infty)$ and $w|_Q\in BV(Q)$ if $p=1$. Assume that
$w\equiv c$ on $Q^\complement\cup\mathcal C_a(Q)$ for some $c\in\mathbb{R}.$
Then, for any positive sequence $\{\lambda_j\}$ with $\lambda_j\to\infty$
if $\gamma\in(0,\infty)$ and $\lambda_j\to0$ if $\gamma\in(-\infty,0)$ as $j\to\infty$, there exists a sequence
$\{W_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^\infty(\mathbb R^N)$ such that
$W_j\to w$ in $L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^N)$ as $j\to\infty$ and
\begin{align}\label{tonghui}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(W_j;\mathbb R^N\right)
\leq\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{Q}(w|_Q).
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Fix $M\in(\|w\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb R^N)},\infty)$ and $\alpha\in(0,1)$.
Let $T_M$ be the truncation operator as in \eqref{jieding}.
By Theorem \ref{thm-domfull} and \eqref{deng}, we conclude that there
exists a sequence $\{v_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^p(Q)$
such that $v_j\to w$ in $L^p(Q)$ as $j\to\infty$ and
\begin{align}\label{yunei}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\alpha\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(T_M v_j;Q\right)
\leq\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\alpha\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(v_j;Q\right)
\leq\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{Q}(w|_Q).
\end{align}
Since $M>\|w\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb R^N)}$, it follows that
\begin{align*}
\left\|T_M v_j-w\right\|_{L^p(Q)}=\left\|T_M v_j-T_M w\right\|_{L^p(Q)}\leq\left\|v_j-w\right\|_{L^p(Q)}\to0
\end{align*}
as $j\to\infty$. Thus, $T_Mv_j\to w$ in $L^p(Q)$ as $j\to\infty$.
Choose a Lipschitz function $\eta:Q\to[0,1]$ such that
$\eta\equiv 0$ on $\mathcal C_{\frac{a}{4}}(Q),$
$\eta\equiv 1$ on $Q\setminus\mathcal C_{\frac{a}{2}}(Q)$,
and $L_\eta\leq\frac{4}{a},$ where $L_\eta$ is its Lipschitz constant.
For any given $j\in\mathbb N$, define $W_j^\alpha$ by setting
\begin{align}\label{pinjie}
W_j^\alpha(x):=
\begin{cases}
\eta(x)T_M v_j(x)+[1-\eta(x)]w(x),&x\in Q,\\
w(x),&x\in Q^\complement.
\end{cases}
\end{align}
Then $W_j^\alpha$ is bounded by $M$ and equals $w$ on
$Q^\complement\cup\mathcal C_{\frac{a}{4}}(Q)$. Moreover,
\begin{align}\label{pinlian}
\left\|W_j^\alpha-w\right\|_{L^p(Q)}\leq\left\|T_M v_j-w\right\|_{L^p(Q)}\to0
\end{align}
as $j\to\infty$. This further implies that $W_j^\alpha\to w$ in $L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb R^N)$ as $j\to\infty$.

Now, we show that
\begin{align}\label{tonggu}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(W_j^\alpha;\mathbb R^N\right)
\leq\alpha^{-p}\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{Q}(w|_Q).
\end{align}
Fix $j\in\mathbb{N}.$ Let
\begin{align*}
D_1:=\left\{(x,y)\in Q\times Q:0<|x-y|<\frac{a}{4}\right\},
\end{align*}
\begin{align*}
D_2:=\left\{(x,y)\in(\mathbb R^N\times\mathbb R^N)
\setminus(Q\times Q):0<|x-y|<\frac{a}{4}\right\},
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
D_3:=\left\{(x,y)\in\mathbb R^N\times\mathbb R^N:|x-y|\geq \frac{a}{4}\right\}.
\end{align*}
Then
\begin{align*}
\left\{(x,y)\in\mathbb R^N\times\mathbb R^N:x\ne y\right\}
=D_1\cup D_2\cup D_3
\end{align*}
and hence
\begin{align}\label{fenjie}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(W_j^\alpha;\mathbb R^N\right)
=\sum_{k=1}^3\lambda_j^p
\underset{|W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)|>
\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}}{\iint_{D_k}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx.
\end{align}
Let $(x,y)\in D_1$. From \eqref{pinjie}, we infer that
\begin{align*}
W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)
&=\eta(x)\left[T_M v_j(x)-T_M v_j(y)\right]
+[1-\eta(x)]\left[w(x)-w(y)\right]\\
&\quad+\left[\eta(x)-\eta(y)\right]\left[T_M v_j(y)-w(y)\right].
\end{align*}
If $1-\eta(x)\ne0$, then $x\in\mathcal C_{\frac{a}{2}}(Q)$ and
$y\in\mathcal C_{\frac{3a}{4}}(Q)$. By the assumption on $w$, we obtain
$w(x)=w(y)=c$. Consequently,
\begin{align*}
[1-\eta(x)]\left[w(x)-w(y)\right]=0.
\end{align*}
From this, we deduce that
\begin{align*}
|W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)|
\leq\left|T_M v_j(x)-T_M v_j(y)\right|+L_\eta|x-y|\,\left|T_M v_j(y)-w(y)\right|,
\end{align*}
which further implies that
\begin{align*}
&\left\{(x,y)\in D_1:\
|W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)|>\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}\right\}\\
&\quad\subset\left\{(x,y)\in Q\times Q:
|T_M v_j(x)-T_M v_j(y)|>\alpha\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}\right\}\\
&\qquad\cup\left\{(x,y)\in Q\times Q:0<|x-y|^\gamma<
\frac{L_\eta^p|T_M v_j(y)-w(y)|^p}{(1-\alpha)^p\lambda_j^p}\right\}.
\end{align*}
Consequently,
\begin{align}\label{e5.6}
&\lambda_j^p\underset{|W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)|>
\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}}
{\iint_{D_1}}|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx\notag\\
&\quad\leq\alpha^{-p}F_{\alpha\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(T_M v_j;Q\right)
+\lambda_j^p\int_Q\int_{\left\{0<|h|^\gamma<
\frac{L_\eta^p|T_M v_j(y)-w(y)|^p}{(1-\alpha)^p\lambda_j^p}\right\}}
|h|^{\gamma-N}\,dh\,dy\notag\\
&\quad=\alpha^{-p}F_{\alpha\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(T_M v_j;Q\right)
+\frac{\mathcal H^{N-1}\left(\mathbb S^{N-1}\right)L_\eta^p}
{|\gamma|(1-\alpha)^p}\left\|T_M v_j-w\right\|_{L^p(Q)}^p.
\end{align}
Letting $j\to\infty$ in \eqref{e5.6} and using \eqref{yunei}, we conclude that
\begin{align}\label{shangu}
&\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}\lambda_j^p\underset{|W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)|>
\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}}
{\iint_{D_1}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx\notag\\
&\quad\leq\alpha^{-p}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\alpha\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(T_M v_j;Q\right)
\leq\alpha^{-p}\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{Q}(w|_Q).
\end{align}
Let  $(x,y)\in D_2$. By symmetry, we can assume that $x\in Q^\complement$.
Since $|x-y|<\frac{a}{4}$, it follows that
$y\in Q^\complement\cup\mathcal C_{\frac{a}{4}}(Q).$
This, together with the definition of $W_j^\alpha$ and the assumption on $w$, implies that
\begin{align*}
W_j^\alpha(x)=W_j^\alpha(y)=c.
\end{align*}
Thus, for any $j\in\mathbb N$,
\begin{align}\label{kualing}
\lambda_j^p\underset{|W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)|>
\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}}{\iint_{D_2}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx=0.
\end{align}
Let $(x,y)\in D_3$. If $\gamma\in(0,\infty)$, then
$\lim_{j\to\infty}\lambda_j(\frac a4)^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}=\infty.$
From this, we infer that, for all sufficiently large $j$,
\begin{align*}
|W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)|
\leq2M\leq\lambda_j\left(\frac a4\right)^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}
\leq\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}},
\end{align*}
which implies that, for all sufficiently large $j$,
\begin{align}\label{shenme}
\lambda_j^p\underset{|W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)|>
\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}}{\iint_{D_3}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx=0.
\end{align}
If $\gamma\in(-\infty,0)$, then $\lim_{j\to\infty}\lambda_j=0$.
Note that, for any $(x,y)\in Q^\complement\times Q^\complement$,
\begin{align*}
W_j^\alpha(x)=W_j^\alpha(y)=w(x)=w(y)=c.
\end{align*}
Thus,
\begin{align}\label{butong}
&\left\{(x,y)\in D_3:|W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)|>
\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}\right\}\notag\\
&\quad\subset D_3\setminus(Q^\complement\times Q^\complement)\notag\\
&\quad\subset\left\{(x,y)\in Q\times\mathbb R^N:|x-y|\geq \frac{a}{4}\right\}
\cup\left\{(x,y)\in\mathbb R^N\times Q:|x-y|\geq \frac{a}{4}\right\}.
\end{align}
Using this, we have
\begin{align*}
&\lambda_j^p\underset{|W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)|>
\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}}{\iint_{D_3}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx\\
&\quad\leq2\lambda_j^p\int_Q\int_{|x-y|\geq \frac{a}{4}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx=2\lambda_j^p\int_Q\int_{|h|\geq \frac{a}{4}}|h|^{\gamma-N}\,dh\,dx\\
&\quad=2\lambda_j^p|Q|\frac{\mathcal H^{N-1}\left(\mathbb S^{N-1}\right)}{|\gamma|}
\left(\frac a4\right)^\gamma\to0
\end{align*}
as $j\to\infty$. By this and \eqref{shenme}, we conclude that
\begin{align*}
\lim_{j\to\infty}\lambda_j^p\underset{|W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)|>
\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}}{\iint_{D_3}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-N}\,dy\,dx=0,
\end{align*}
which, combined with \eqref{shangu}, \eqref{kualing} and \eqref{fenjie},
implies that \eqref{tonggu} holds.

Finally,  we remove $\alpha$. Choose $\{\alpha_n\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset (0,1)$ such that $\lim_{n\to\infty}\alpha_n=1.$ From \eqref{pinlian} and \eqref{tonggu},
we deduce that, for any given $n\in\mathbb{N},$ there exists a
integer $J_n$ such that, for any $j\geq J_n$,
\begin{align}\label{kun1}
\left\|W_j^{\alpha_n}-w\right\|_{L^p(Q)}<\frac1n
\end{align}
and
\begin{align}\label{kun2}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(W_j^{\alpha_n};\mathbb R^N\right)
\leq\alpha_n^{-p}\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{Q}(w|_Q)+\frac1n.
\end{align}
Without loss of generality, we may assume that $\{J_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ is increasing. Define
\begin{align*}
W_j:=\begin{cases}
w,&j<J_1,\\
W_j^{\alpha_n},&J_n\leq j<J_{n+1}, n\in\mathbb{N}.
\end{cases}
\end{align*}
Then $|W_j|\leq M$ and $W_j=w$ almost everywhere on
$Q^\complement\cup\mathcal C_{\frac{a}{4}}(Q)$.
By this, \eqref{kun1} and \eqref{kun2}, we find that,
for any $j\in\mathbb{N}$ with $J_n\leq j<J_{n+1}$ for some $n\in\mathbb{N}$,
\begin{align*}
\|W_j-w\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^N)}\leq |Q|^{1-\frac{1}{p}}
\left\|W_j^{\alpha_n}-w\right\|_{L^p(Q)}<\frac{|Q|^{1-\frac{1}{p}}}n
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(W_j;\mathbb R^N\right)
\leq\left(1-\frac1n\right)^{-p}\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{Q}(w|_Q)+\frac1n,
\end{align*}
which further implies that
$W_j\to w$ in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^N)$ as $j\to\infty$ and
\eqref{tonghui} holds. This completes the proof of Lemma \ref{lem-oneconst}.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- lemma-20: body_md -->
\label{lem-twoconst}
Let $p, R, a, Q,$ and $\mathcal{C}_a(Q)$ be as in Lemma \ref{lem-oneconst}
with $N=1$, and assume that
$\gamma\in(-\infty,-1)\cup(0,\infty)$.
Suppose that $w\in L^\infty(\mathbb R)$ satisfying $w|_Q\in W^{1,p}(Q)$
if $p\in(1,\infty)$ and $w|_Q\in BV(Q)$ if $p=1$. Assume that
there exist $c_-,c_+\in\mathbb{R}$ such that
$w\equiv c_-$ on $(-\infty,-R+a)$ and $w\equiv c_+$ on $(R-a,\infty)$.
Then, for any positive sequence $\{\lambda_j\}$ with $\lambda_j\to\infty$
if $\gamma\in(0,\infty)$ and $\lambda_j\to0$ if $\gamma\in(-\infty,0)$ as
$j\to\infty$, there exists a sequence $\{W_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^\infty(\mathbb R)$ such that
$W_j\to w$ in $L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R})$ as $j\to\infty$ and
\begin{align*}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}(W_j;\mathbb R)
\leq\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{Q}(w|_Q).
\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-20 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
The proof is similar to that of Lemma \ref{lem-oneconst}, except for the estimate of \eqref{butong}.
By the assumption of $w$, we find that, for any $(x,y)\in(-\infty,-R]\times(-\infty,-R]$,
\begin{align*}
W_j^\alpha(x)=W_j^\alpha(y)=w(x)=w(y)=c_-,
\end{align*}
and, for any $(x,y)\in[R,\infty)\times[R,\infty)$,
\begin{align*}
W_j^\alpha(x)=W_j^\alpha(y)=w(x)=w(y)=c_+.
\end{align*}
Thus,
\begin{align*}
&\left\{(x,y)\in D_3:\left|W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)\right|>
\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}\right\}\\
&\quad\subset\left\{(x,y)\in Q\times\mathbb R:|x-y|\geq \frac{a}{4}\right\}
\cup\left\{(x,y)\in Q^\complement\times Q:|x-y|\geq \frac{a}{4}\right\}\\
&\qquad\cup\left((-\infty,-R]\times[R,\infty)\right)
\cup\left([R,\infty)\times(-\infty,-R]\right).
\end{align*}
Using this, we further obtain
\begin{align*}
&\lambda_j^p\underset{|W_j^\alpha(x)-W_j^\alpha(y)|>
\lambda_j|x-y|^{1+\frac{\gamma}{p}}}{\iint_{D_3}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-1}\,dy\,dx\\
&\quad\leq2\lambda_j^p\int_Q\int_{|x-y|\geq \frac{a}{4}}
|x-y|^{\gamma-1}\,dy\,dx
+2\lambda_j^p\int_{-\infty}^{-R}\int_R^\infty
(y-x)^{\gamma-1}\,dy\,dx\\
&\quad=\frac{4|Q|}{|\gamma|}\left(\frac a4\right)^\gamma\lambda_j^p
+\frac{2(2R)^{\gamma+1}}{\gamma(\gamma+1)}\lambda_j^p\to0,
\end{align*}
as $j\to\infty$, where, in the last step, we used
the range $\gamma\in (-\infty,-1)\cup(0,\infty).$
This completes the proof of \Cref{lem-twoconst}.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- lemma-21: body_md -->
\label{lem-sobloc}
\begin{enumerate}[{\rm(i)}]
\item
Let $N\in\mathbb{N}$, $p\in(1,\infty)$ and $u\in\dot W^{1,p}(\mathbb R^N)$. Then there exist
$\{\phi_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C_{\rm c}^\infty(\mathbb R^N)$ and
$\{c_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb R$ such that
\begin{align}\label{tiding}
v_m\to u\quad\text{in }L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)
\ \ \text{and}\ \ \nabla v_m\to\nabla u\quad\text{in }L^p(\mathbb R^N),
\end{align}
with $v_m:=c_m+\phi_m$ for any $m\in\mathbb N$, as $m\to\infty.$

\item Let $N\geq2$ and $u\in\dot{BV}(\mathbb R^N)$. Then there exist
$\{\phi_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C_{\rm c}^\infty(\mathbb R^N)$ and
$\{c_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb R$ such that
\begin{align}\label{bianding}
v_m\to u\quad\text{in }L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)
\ \ \text{and}\ \
|Dv_m|(\mathbb R^N)\to|Du|(\mathbb R^N),
\end{align}
with $v_m:=c_m+\phi_m$ for any $m\in\mathbb N$, as $m\to\infty$.

\item Let $u\in\dot{BV}(\mathbb R)$. Then there exist bounded functions
$\{v_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\dot{BV}(\mathbb R)$ such that
\begin{align*}
v_m\to u\quad\text{in }L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R)\ \ \text{and}\ \
|Dv_m|(\mathbb R)\to|Du|(\mathbb R)
\end{align*}
as $m\to\infty$.
Moreover, there exist constants
$\{c_m^-\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}},\{c_m^+\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb R$ such that, for any $m\in\mathbb{N},$
$v_m\equiv c_m^{-}$ on $(-\infty,-m]$ and
$v_m\equiv c_m^{+}$ on $[m,\infty)$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-21 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
We first show (i). By \cite[Theorem~4]{hk95}, we find that there exist
$\{\phi_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C_{\rm c}^\infty(\mathbb R^N)$ such that
\begin{align*}
\|\nabla\phi_m-\nabla u\|_{L^p(\mathbb R^N)}\to0
\end{align*}
as $m\to\infty$. Let $R\in (1,\infty)$ and $B_R:=B(\mathbf{0},R)$.
For  any $m\in\mathbb{N},$ define $c_m:=(u-\phi_m)_{B_1}$ and $v_m:=c_m+\phi_m$.
Then, from Poincar\'e's inequality,  we deduce that
\begin{align}\label{ji}
\|v_m-u-(v_m-u)_{B_R}\|_{L^p(B_R)}\leq C_R\|\nabla v_m-\nabla u\|_{L^p(B_R)}\to 0
\end{align}
as $m\to\infty,$
where $C_R$ is a positive constant depending only on $R.$
Applying Poincar\'e's inequality again and  using the assumption that
$(v_m-u)_{B_1}=0$, we obtain, for any $m\in\mathbb{N},$
\begin{align}\label{wen}
\left|(v_m-u)_{B_R}\right|
&=\left|\frac1{|B_1|}\int_{B_1}\left((v_m-u)_{B_R}-v_m(x)+u(x)\right)\,dx\right|\notag\\
&\leq C_R\left\|v_m-u-(v_m-u)_{B_R}\right\|_{L^p(B_R)}
\leq C_R\left\|\nabla v_m-\nabla u\right\|_{L^p(B_R)}\to 0
\end{align}
as $m\to\infty.$ By this and \eqref{ji}, we conclude that \eqref{tiding} holds.
This completes the proof of  (i).

Next, we prove (ii). Let $\rho\in C_{\rm c}^\infty(B_1)$ be nonnegative with
$\int_{\mathbb R^N}\rho\,dx=1$.
For any $m\in\mathbb{N}$, let $u_m:=\rho_{\frac{1}{m}}*u$.
Then, for any $m\in\mathbb{N},$
$u_m\in C^\infty(\mathbb R^N)\cap\dot W^{1,1}(\mathbb R^N)$,
\begin{align}\label{ki}
\|\nabla u_m\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^N)}
\leq|Du|(\mathbb R^N),
\end{align}
and  $u_m\to u$ in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$ as $m\to\infty$.\
From the assumption that $N\geq2$ and  \cite[Theorem~4]{hk95}, we infer that
there exist $\{\phi_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C_{\rm c}^\infty(\mathbb R^N)$ such that, for any $m\in\mathbb N$,
\begin{align}\label{fuk}
\|\nabla\phi_m-\nabla u_m\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^N)}<\frac1m.
\end{align}
For any $m\in\mathbb{N}$, define $c_m:=(u_m-\phi_m)_{B_1}$
and $v_m:=c_m+\phi_m$.
By an argument similar to that used in \eqref{wen} and \eqref{ji}, we find that,
for any $m\in\mathbb{N},$
\begin{align*}
\left\|v_m-u_m-(v_m-u_m)_{B_R}\right\|_{L^1(B_R)}\lesssim
\left\|\nabla v_m-\nabla u_m\right\|_{L^1(B_R)}\leq \frac{1}{m}
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
\left|(v_m-u_m)_{B_R}\right|\lesssim \frac{1}{m}.
\end{align*}
This, combined with the fact that
$u_m\to u$ in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$ as $m\to\infty$,
implies that  $v_m\to u$ in $L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^N)$ as $m\to\infty.$
Moreover, using \eqref{fuk} and \eqref{ki}, we obtain, for any $m\in\mathbb{N},$
\begin{align*}
|Dv_m|(\mathbb R^N)=\|\nabla\phi_m\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^N)}
\leq\|\nabla u_m\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^N)}+\frac1m\leq |Du|(\mathbb R^N)+\frac1m.
\end{align*}
Thus,
\begin{align*}
|Du|(B_R)\leq\varliminf_{m\to\infty}|Dv_m|(B_R)
\leq\varliminf_{m\to\infty}|Dv_m|(\mathbb R^N)\leq\varlimsup_{m\to\infty}|Dv_m|(\mathbb R^N)\leq |Du|(\mathbb R^N).
\end{align*}
Letting $R\to\infty$ in the above inequality, we conclude that
\eqref{bianding} holds. This completes the proof of (ii).

Finally, we show (iii). From \cite[Theorem~3.28]{afp00}, it follows that
$u$ have one-side limits at every point, which are denoted by $u(x-)$ and $u(x+)$. Moreover, for any given $a,b\in\mathbb{R}$ with $a<b$ and almost every $x\in (a,b),$
\begin{align}\label{qiutian}
u(x)=u(a+)+Du((a,x])
\end{align}
and
\begin{align}\label{tianqiu}
u(b-)-u(a+)=Du((a,b)).
\end{align}
For any $m\in\mathbb{N}$, let $c_m^-:=u(-m+)$ and $c_m^+:=u(m-)$.
For any given $m\in\mathbb N$ and any $x\in\mathbb R$, define
\begin{align*}
v_m(x):=
\begin{cases}
c_m^-,&x\in(-\infty,-m],\\
u(x),& x\in(-m,m),\\
c_m^+,&x\in[m,\infty).
\end{cases}
\end{align*}
By \eqref{qiutian} and \eqref{tianqiu}, we find that, for any $m\in\mathbb N$,
\begin{align*}
\sup_{x\in\mathbb R}|v_m(x)|\leq |u(-m+)|+|Du|((-m,m))<\infty.
\end{align*}
Thus, for any given $m\in\mathbb N$, $v_m\in L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R)$
is bounded. Moreover, it is obviously that
$v_m\to u$ in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R)$ as $m\to\infty.$
Note that, for any $m\in\mathbb{N}$ and $\varphi\in C^\infty_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R}),$
\begin{align*}
-\int_{\mathbb R}v_m(x)\varphi'(x)\,dx&=-c_m^-\int_{-\infty}^{-m}\varphi'(x)\,dx
-\int_{-m}^{m}u(x)\varphi'(x)\,dx
-c_m^+\int_m^\infty\varphi'(x)\,dx\\
&=-c_m^-\varphi(-m)
+\int_{(-m,m)}\varphi\,dDu
+u(-m+)\varphi(-m)-u(m-)\varphi(m)
+c_m^+\varphi(m)\\
&=\int_{(-m,m)}\varphi\,dDu,
\end{align*}
which implies that, for any given $m\in\mathbb{N},$
$Dv_m=Du\mathopen{\scalebox{1.6}{$\llcorner$}}(-m,m)$
and hence $|Dv_m|=|Du|\mathopen{\scalebox{1.6}{$\llcorner$}}(-m,m)$.
This further yields
\begin{align*}
\lim_{m\to\infty}|Dv_m|(\mathbb R)=\lim_{m\to\infty}|Du|((-m,m))=|Du|(\mathbb R).
\end{align*}
This completes the proof of (iii) and hence Lemma \ref{lem-sobloc}.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Fix a sequence $\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,\infty)$ such that
$\lambda_j\to\infty$ if $\gamma\in(0,\infty)$ and $\lambda_j\to0$ if $\gamma\in(-\infty,-1]$ as $j\to\infty$. By Proposition \ref{prop-whllower},
we obtain the desired $\Gamma$-liminf  inequality.
It remains to show that, for any $u\in L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$,
there exists a sequence $\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}$ such that
$u_j\to u$ in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$ as $j\to\infty$
and
\begin{align*}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j;\mathbb R^N\right)
\leq\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}(u).
\end{align*}
If $\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}(u)=\infty$, then there is nothing to prove.
We now assume $\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}(u)<\infty$.
From Lemma \ref{lem-sobloc}, it follows that there exists a sequence
$\{w_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset L^\infty(\mathbb R^N)$ such that
\begin{align}\label{nengjin}
w_m\to u\quad\text{in }L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)
\ \ \text{and}\ \
\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}(w_m)\to\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}(u)
\end{align}
as $m\to\infty$. For any given $m\in\mathbb{N},$ choosing $r_{m}$ sufficiently
large and applying Lemmas \ref{lem-oneconst} and \ref{lem-twoconst} with
$Q_{m}:=(-r_m,r_m)^N$, we conclude that there exists a sequence
$\{W_j^{(m)}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset L^\infty(\mathbb R^N)$ such that
\begin{align}\label{whole-fixed-local}
W_j^{(m)}\to w_m\quad\text{in }L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)
\end{align}
as $j\to\infty$ and
\begin{align}\label{whole-fixed-upper}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(W_j^{(m)};\mathbb R^N\right)
\leq \Psi_{p,\gamma}^{Q_m}\left(w_m|_{Q_m}\right)=\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}(w_m).
\end{align}
By \eqref{whole-fixed-local} and \eqref{whole-fixed-upper},
we find that, for any given $m\in\mathbb{N}$, there exists an integer $J_m$
such that, for any $j\geq J_m$,
\begin{align}\label{whole-diagonal-local}
\left\|W_j^{(m)}-w_m\right\|_{L^1((-m,m)^N)}<\frac1m
\end{align}
and
\begin{align}\label{whole-diagonal-upper}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(W_j^{(m)};\mathbb R^N\right)
\leq\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}(w_m)+\frac1m.
\end{align}
Without loss of generality, we may assume that $\{J_m\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}$ is strictly increasing.
For any $j\in\mathbb N$, define
\begin{align*}
u_j:=
\begin{cases}
w_1,&j<J_1,\\
W_j^{(m)},&J_m\leq j<J_{m+1},\ m\in\mathbb{N}.
\end{cases}
\end{align*}
From \eqref{whole-diagonal-local} and \eqref{whole-diagonal-upper},
we infer that, for any fixed $R\in (0,\infty)$ and any $j\in\mathbb{N}$ with
$J_m\leq j<J_{m+1}$ for some $m\in[R,\infty)\cap\mathbb N$,
\begin{align*}
\|u_j-u\|_{L^1((-R,R)^N)}&\leq\left\|W_j^{(m)}-w_m\right\|_{L^1((-R,R)^N)}
+\left\|w_m-u\right\|_{L^1((-R,R)^N)}\\
&<\frac1m+\left\|w_m-u\right\|_{L^1((-R,R)^N)}
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align}\label{e5.30}
F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j;\mathbb R^N\right)\leq\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}(w_m)+\frac1m.
\end{align}
Letting $j\to\infty$ in \eqref{e5.30} and using \eqref{nengjin}, we conclude that
$u_j\to u$ in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R^N)$ and
\begin{align*}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,p,\gamma}\left(u_j;\mathbb R^N\right)
\leq\Psi_{p,\gamma}^{\rm hom}(u).
\end{align*}
This completes the proof of Theorem \ref{thm-whlndeg}.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{thm-whlendpt}
The family $F_{\lambda,1,-1}(\cdot;\mathbb R)$ converges, in the sense of
$\Gamma$-convergence in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R)$, to $\Psi_{1,-1}^{\rm hom}$ as $\lambda\to0^+$.
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- proposition-9: body_md -->
\label{prop-closinf}
Let $\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy
$\lambda_j\to0$ as $j\to\infty$, $u\in L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R)$, and
$\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R)$ satisfy
$u_j\to u$ in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R)$ as $j\to\infty$.
If
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}(u_j;\mathbb R)<\infty,
\end{align*}
then $u\in\dot{BV}(\mathbb R)$ and
\begin{align}\label{bixia}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(u_j;\mathbb R\right)\geq C_{1,1,-1}^{\rm cell}\left[|Du|(\mathbb R)+|u_+-u_-|\right].
\end{align}
<!-- end proposition-9 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
By Proposition \ref{prop-whllower}, we find that $u\in\dot{BV}(\mathbb R)$.
Passing to a subsequence, we may assume that
\begin{align*}
L:=\lim_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(u_j;\mathbb R\right)
=\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(u_j;\mathbb R\right)<\infty.
\end{align*}
Fix $M\in(\|u\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb R)},\infty)$. Let $R\in (0,\infty)$ and
$E_R:=(-\infty,-R)\cup(R,\infty).$
Then, for any $j\in\mathbb{N},$
\begin{align*}
F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(u_j;\mathbb R\right)
\geq F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(u_j;(-R,R)\right)
+F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(u_j;E_R\right).
\end{align*}
Using  Theorem \ref{thm-domfull}(ii), we have
\begin{align*}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(u_j;(-R,R)\right)
\geq C_{1,1,-1}^{\rm cell}|Du|((-R,R)).
\end{align*}
Thus, to show \eqref{bixia}, it remains to prove that
\begin{align}\label{waixia}
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(u_j;E_R\right)
\geq C_{1,1,-1}^{\rm cell}|u_+-u_-|.
\end{align}
Let $T_M$  be as in \eqref{jieding}. For any $0<|s|<1/R$, let
\begin{align*}
v_j(s):=T_M(u_j(1/s))\quad\text{and}\quad v(s):=u(1/s),
\end{align*}
and let $v_j(0)=v(0)=0$. Then, for any $\delta\in (R,\infty)$,
\begin{align*}
\int_{1/\delta<|s|<1/R}|v_j(s)-v(s)|\,ds&=\int_{R<|x|<\delta}|T_M(u_j(x))-T_M(u(x))|\,\frac{dx}{x^2}\\
&\leq\frac1{R^2}\int_{-\delta}^{\delta}|u_j(x)-u(x)|\,dx
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
\int_{|s|<1/\delta}|v_j(s)-v(s)|\,ds\leq\frac{4M}{\delta}.
\end{align*}
Thus, for any $\delta\in (R,\infty)$,
\begin{align}\label{e5.33}
\left\|v_j-v\right\|_{L^1((-1/R,1/R))}\leq\frac1{R^2}
\left\|u_j-u\right\|_{L^1((-\delta,\delta))}+\frac{4M}{\delta}.
\end{align}
Letting $j,\delta\to\infty$ in \eqref{e5.33}, we conclude that
\begin{align}\label{fanlian}
v_j\to v\quad\text{in }L^1((-1/R,1/R)).
\end{align}
From the definition of $F_{\lambda,1,-1}$, we deduce that, for any $j\in\mathbb{N},$
\begin{align}\label{e5.34}
&F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(v_j;(-1/R,1/R)\right)\notag\\
&\quad=\lambda_j\underset{|T_M(u_j(1/s))-T_M(u_j(1/t))|>\lambda_j}
{\int_{0<|s|<1/R}\int_{0<|t|<1/R}}\frac{dt\,ds}{|s-t|^2}=\lambda_j
\underset{|T_M(u_j(x))-T_M(u_j(y))|>\lambda_j}
{\int_{E_R}\int_{E_R}}\frac{dy\,dx}{|x-y|^2}\notag\\
&\quad=F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(T_M(u_j);E_R\right)\leq
F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(u_j;E_R\right)\leq
F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(u_j;\mathbb R\right).
\end{align}
Letting $j\to\infty$ in \eqref{e5.34} and using Theorem \ref{thm-domfull}(ii)
and \eqref{fanlian}, we then conclude that $v\in BV((-1/R,1/R))$ and
\begin{align}\label{chaoji}
C_{1,1,-1}^{\rm cell}|Dv|((-1/R,1/R))
&\leq\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(v_j;(-1/R,1/R)\right)\notag\\
&\leq\varliminf_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(u_j;E_R\right)\leq L<\infty.
\end{align}
On the other hand, we find that
\begin{align*}
v(0-)=\lim_{s\to0-}u(1/s)=u_-
\ \ \text{and}\ \ v(0+)=\lim_{s\to0+}u(1/s)=u_+,
\end{align*}
which, together with \cite[Theorem~3.28]{afp00}, implies that
\begin{align*}
|Dv|((-1/R,1/R))\geq |Dv|(\left\{0\right\})=|Dv(\left\{0\right\})|=|u_+-u_-|.
\end{align*}
From  this and \eqref{chaoji}, we infer that \eqref{waixia} holds, which
completes the proof of Proposition \ref{prop-closinf}.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- proposition-10: body_md -->
\label{prop-clossup}
Let $u\in\dot{BV}(\mathbb R)$ and $\{\lambda_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset(0,\infty)$ satisfy $\lambda_j\to0$ as $j\to\infty$.
Then there exists a sequence $\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R)$ such that $u_j\to u$ in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R)$ as $j\to\infty$ and
\begin{align}\label{bishang}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(u_j;\mathbb R\right)
\leq C_{1,1,-1}^{\rm cell}\left[|Du|(\mathbb R)+|u_+-u_-|\right].
\end{align}
<!-- end proposition-10 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
For any given $m\in\mathbb{N}$ and any $x\in\mathbb R$, define
\begin{align*}
w_m(x):=
\begin{cases}
u(x),&x\in(-m,m),\\
u(-m+),&x\notin(-m,m).
\end{cases}
\end{align*}
Obviously, $w_m\to u$ in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R)$ as $m\to\infty$. Furthermore,
by \eqref{qiutian}, we find that
\begin{align*}
\|w_m\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb R)}\leq |u(-m+)|+|Du|((-m,m))<\infty.
\end{align*}
Note that, for any $m\in\mathbb{N}$ and $\varphi\in C_{\rm c}^\infty(\mathbb R)$,
\begin{align*}
&-\int_{\mathbb R}w_m(x)\varphi'(x)\,dx\\
&\quad=-u(-m+)\int_{-\infty}^{-m}\varphi'(x)\,dx
-\int_{-m}^{m}u(x)\varphi'(x)\,dx
-u(-m+)\int_m^\infty\varphi'(x)\,dx\\
&\quad=-u(-m+)\varphi(-m)+\int_{(-m,m)}\varphi\,dDu
+u(-m+)\varphi(-m)-u(m-)\varphi(m)+u(-m+)\varphi(m)\\
&\quad=\int_{(-m,m)}\varphi\,dDu+\left[u(-m+)-u(m-)\right]\varphi(m).
\end{align*}
This shows that, for any $m\in\mathbb{N},$
\begin{align*}
Dw_m=Du\mathopen{\scalebox{1.6}{$\llcorner$}}(-m,m)+
\left[u(-m+)-u(m-)\right]\delta_m
\end{align*}
and hence
\begin{align*}
|Dw_m|(\mathbb R)=|Du|((-m,m))+|u(-m+)-u(m-)|,
\end{align*}
where $\delta_m$ is the unit Dirac mass at $m$.
Letting $m\to\infty,$ we have
\begin{align*}
\lim_{m\to\infty}|Dw_m|(\mathbb R)
=|Du|(\mathbb R)+|u_+-u_-|.
\end{align*}
For any given $m\in\mathbb{N},$
choosing $r_{m}$ sufficiently  large and applying
Lemma \ref{lem-oneconst} with
$p:=1$, $\gamma:=-1,$ and
$Q_{m}:=(-r_m,r_m)^N$, we conclude that
there exists  a sequence
$\{W_j^{(m)}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\subset L^\infty(\mathbb R^N)$ such that
$W_j^{(m)}\to w_m$ in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R)$ as $j\to\infty$ and
\begin{align*}
\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}F_{\lambda_j,1,-1}\left(W_j^{(m)};\mathbb R\right)
\leq\Psi_{1,-1}^{Q_m}(w_m|_{Q_m})=C_{1,1,-1}^{\rm cell}|Dw_m|(\mathbb R).
\end{align*}
Using this and a diagonal argument used in the proof of Theorem \ref{thm-whlndeg}, we find that there exists a sequence
$\{u_j\}_{j\in\mathbb N}\subset L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R)$ such that $u_j\to u$ in $L^1_{\rm loc}(\mathbb R)$ as $j\to\infty$ and \eqref{bishang} holds.
This completes the proof of Proposition \ref{prop-clossup}.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
